## [First Synthetic Cell That Grows and Divides Created](https://www.quantamagazine.org/for-the-first-time-a-cell-built-from-scratch-grows-and-divides-20260701/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers led by Dr. Kate Adamala at Biotic have built the first synthetic cell from scratch that can grow, replicate its DNA, and divide, bypassing the need for a cytoskeleton. This breakthrough overcomes a major hurdle in synthetic biology, bringing us closer to creating artificial life and enabling applications in medicine, materials, and biotechnology. The synthetic cells, called SpudCells, use a simplified division mechanism that does not rely on a cytoskeleton, and the work has been published as a 190-page manuscript, though it faced initial rejection from Cell.

hackernews · defrost · Jul 1, 14:20 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48747304)

**Background**: Natural cells use a cytoskeleton—a network of protein fibers—to reorganize and split during division. Synthetic biologists had struggled to replicate this complex process. Adamala's team designed SpudCells to divide without a cytoskeleton, using a different physical mechanism.

## [Box3D: New Open-Source 3D Physics Engine by Box2D Creator](https://box2d.org/posts/2026/06/announcing-box3d/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Erin Catto, the creator of the widely-used Box2D physics engine, has announced Box3D, a new open-source 3D physics engine. The announcement was made on the Box2D blog in June 2026. Box3D brings the proven design philosophy and robustness of Box2D into the 3D realm, potentially becoming a foundational tool for indie game development, robotics simulation, and machine learning environments. Its open-source nature under the MIT license ensures broad accessibility and community-driven improvements. The announcement did not include specific technical details about determinism, which is a key concern for networked multiplayer games. The engine focuses on rigid body physics, building on the legacy of Box2D which has been used in popular games like Angry Birds and Limbo.

hackernews · makepanic · Jul 1, 12:12 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48745445)

**Background**: Box2D is a 2D physics engine created by Erin Catto and used in numerous games and simulations. It is known for its efficiency, stability, and ease of use, and has been a staple in indie game development. Box3D extends this concept to three dimensions, aiming to provide a similar level of quality for 3D physics simulation.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [New AGI Framework Uses Sparse Binary Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31819) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper on arXiv proposes a computational framework for AGI based on sparse binary representations and topological plasticity, achieving constant-time associative memory. This framework could shift the AI paradigm from continuous weight adjustments to discrete logic, potentially enabling human-level energy efficiency and a unified theory of cognition. The framework uses subset pattern matching and exact nearest-neighbor search for information retrieval, and maps to neuroanatomy by proposing that the cerebellum and neocortex implement variants of this algorithm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional neural networks rely on continuous weights and matrix multiplication, which are computationally expensive. Sparse binary representations, where only a small fraction of bits are active, are more efficient and biologically plausible. Hyperdimensional computing uses high-dimensional vectors for cognitive tasks, and topological plasticity modifies network structure rather than weights.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Containment Verification: AI Safety Without Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09045) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced containment verification, a method that provably enforces safety boundaries in agentic frameworks by formally verifying a containment layer, independent of AI model alignment. They demonstrated it on PocketFlow using Dafny and forward-simulation refinement. This shifts AI safety guarantees from unverifiable model behavior to the verifiable software framework, potentially making safety assurances robust even as AI capabilities scale. It could fundamentally change how safety is approached in agentic systems. Under havoc oracle semantics, the AI is modeled as an unconstrained oracle over the typed action space, and the containment layer must enforce boundary policies for every possible output. The method uses Dafny for mechanized proof and an agentic synthesis pipeline to avoid tautological specifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic frameworks are software layers that allow AI agents to interact with the world. Traditional safety methods intervene on the model itself, relying on unverifiable properties of learned behavior. Containment verification instead places safety guarantees in the framework's containment layer, which can be formally verified independently of the AI's alignment.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [FFmpeg 9.1 Introduces Major AAC Encoder Improvements](https://hydrogenaudio.org/index.php/topic,129691.0.html) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FFmpeg 9.1 includes a significantly improved native AAC encoder that now supports Perceptual Noise Substitution (PNS), Temporal Noise Shaping (TNS), Intensity Stereo (I/S) coding, and long-term prediction, addressing long-standing quality issues such as chirping artifacts. This update makes FFmpeg's AAC encoder competitive with proprietary alternatives like Apple's Core Audio encoder, eliminating the need for users to install external codecs for high-quality AAC encoding in video production and audio archiving. The new encoder implements PNS, which replaces noisy frequency bands with a single energy scalefactor, improving coding efficiency at low bitrates. TNS is included but disabled by default as it is still in a less-than-usable state. The encoder also works around a bug in FFmpeg's AAC decoder related to stereo PNS.

hackernews · ledoge · Jul 1, 14:10 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48747116)

**Background**: AAC (Advanced Audio Coding) is a widely used lossy audio compression format, but FFmpeg's native encoder historically produced poor quality with artifacts like chirping. Users often relied on external encoders such as Apple's Core Audio or Fraunhofer FDK AAC. Opus is a newer, open codec that generally outperforms AAC at equivalent bitrates.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://ffmpeg.org/index.html#aac_encoder_stable">FFmpeg</a></li>
<li><a href="https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-codecs.html">FFmpeg Codecs Documentation</a></li>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comparison_of_audio_coding_formats">Comparison of audio coding formats - Wikipedia</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community members expressed excitement about the improvements, with some noting that Opus still outperforms AAC at low bitrates. Users who previously had to install Core Audio for decent AAC quality are eager to test the new encoder. There is also discussion about lingering bugs in FFmpeg's AAC decoder.

**Tags**: `#FFmpeg`, `#AAC`, `#audio encoding`, `#open source`, `#codec`

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [Sony to End Physical Game Disc Production by 2028](https://blog.playstation.com/2026/07/01/physical-disc-production-ending-in-january-2028-for-new-games-releasing-on-playstation-consoles/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Sony announced that starting January 2028, it will discontinue production of physical game discs for all new games released on PlayStation consoles, marking a complete shift to digital distribution. This decision signals the end of an era for physical media in gaming, raising serious concerns about digital ownership, game preservation, and consumer rights, as digital purchases are often revocable licenses rather than true ownership. The announcement comes amid Sony's recent removal of hundreds of purchased movies from customer libraries without refund, highlighting the fragility of digital ownership. The move also threatens the viability of Blu-ray disc production, which relies on gaming as a key revenue source.

hackernews · Tiberium · Jul 1, 12:13 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48745456)

**Background**: Physical game discs have been a cornerstone of console gaming since the 1990s, allowing players to own, trade, and resell games. However, digital distribution has grown rapidly due to convenience and lower costs for publishers. The shift raises preservation issues, as digital games can become unplayable when servers shut down or companies go out of business.

<ul>

## [Study: Self-Feedback Adds Little Beyond Unguided Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30774) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces a controlled student-teacher protocol to disentangle feedback-specific gains from other factors in multi-turn language agent improvement, finding that self-generated feedback adds little beyond unguided self-refinement while strong external teachers yield substantial gains. This challenges common assumptions about the effectiveness of self-feedback in AI alignment and agent design, suggesting that the ability to act on feedback, not merely its availability, is the central bottleneck for interactive improvement. The study evaluated thirteen open-weight models across four benchmarks (Omni-MATH, Codeforces, BBEH Linguini, ARC-AGI1) and compared external feedback, self-feedback, and unguided self-refinement while varying interaction history, task difficulty, and teacher access to privileged information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-turn language agent settings, agents can improve over repeated attempts, but it is unclear whether gains come from feedback or from other factors like resampling or additional computation. Self-refinement techniques, where an AI critiques and revises its own outputs, have become popular, but their effectiveness relative to external feedback is debated.

Researchers introduced BayesBench, a benchmark suite with three tasks (Bayesian estimation, prediction, and latent-framed prediction) to evaluate how closely LLMs' belief updates match Bayesian reasoning across multi-turn evidence accumulation. This addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by focusing on the process of belief updating rather than just final answers, which is essential for deploying LLMs in multi-turn conversational agents that must rationally integrate new evidence. ## [LearnStop: Cost-Aware Early Exits for Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30852) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces LearnStop, a learned early-exit mechanism for reasoning language models that uses online features like answer confidence and entropy to decide when to stop. It finds that learned stopping improves over simple thresholds on free-form math tasks but not on multiple-choice or very hard settings. This work provides a cost-aware framework for dynamically allocating computation in reasoning models, which is crucial for reducing inference costs in large language models. The task-dependent findings guide practitioners on when to adopt learned early exits versus simpler scalar rules. LearnStop probes a short answer from the reasoning prefix at fixed budget checkpoints and predicts correctness using features like answer stability and backtracking-marker density. Across 18 task-model settings, the method achieved a post-hoc peak adapt gain of +0.157 on GSM8K with Qwen3-32B, but on multiple-choice tasks scalar exits were competitive or stronger.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Reasoning models are LLMs trained to generate intermediate reasoning steps before producing a final answer, often requiring more computation. Early exit mechanisms aim to reduce inference cost by stopping computation when a confident prediction is reached, but it is unclear when learned exits outperform simple confidence or convergence thresholds.

A new paper introduces CoPref, a framework for AI agents to help users construct preferences through dialog, and CoShop, a benchmark for evaluating such agents in recommender systems. Tests show no frontier agent exceeds 56% accuracy on CoShop despite five interaction turns. This challenges the common assumption that users have well-formed preferences, which is unrealistic for complex domains. The work could lead to more effective AI assistants that educate users rather than just querying them, improving outcomes in recommendation and decision-making systems. CoPref draws on the Search-Experience-Credence framework from Information Economics to model how users construct preferences based on agent dialog actions. The CoShop benchmark simulates an agent conversing with a CoPref user and making recommendations, with accuracy as the metric.

However, users often lack domain knowledge to specify preferences, needing the agent to help them learn through examples or explanations. The Search-Experience-Credence framework classifies product attributes based on how consumers evaluate them: search (evaluated before purchase), experience (after purchase), and credence (even after purchase, difficult to evaluate).

## [RoPoLL: Robust Panel of LLM Judges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30931) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RoPoLL replaces the standard mean aggregation in Panel of LLM Evaluators (PoLL) with the geometric median, a robust mean estimator, to eliminate unbounded bias caused by a single contaminated judge. This provides a theoretically grounded, tuning-free solution to a critical flaw in LLM evaluation, potentially making LLM-as-judge evaluations more reliable and trustworthy for the AI community. RoPoLL achieves the optimal finite-sample breakdown point of 1/2, and experiments show it dominates PoLL on biased corruption types by about 19% on cross-dimensional attacks and by orders of magnitude on heavy-tailed Byzantine adversaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: The Panel of LLM Evaluators (PoLL) uses multiple LLM judges and averages their scores to evaluate other LLMs. However, under the Huber contamination model, even a single biased judge (e.g., due to mode collapse or sycophancy) can cause unbounded bias in the average. The geometric median is a classic robust statistic that resists outliers up to a breakdown point of 1/2.

## [HyPOLE: Hyperproperty-Guided MARL Under Partial Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30966) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HyPOLE introduces a novel framework that uses hyperproperties, specifically HyperLTL temporal logic, to guide multi-agent reinforcement learning under partial observability, and integrates Centralized Training for Decentralized Execution (CTDE) to synthesize decentralized policies. It demonstrates clear advantages over baselines on SMAC, MessySMAC, and WildFire benchmarks. This work bridges formal methods and MARL, offering a mathematically rigorous way to specify objectives and constraints beyond traditional reward shaping. It could enable more reliable and interpretable multi-agent coordination in complex, real-world scenarios like robotics and autonomous systems. HyPOLE leverages HyperLTL, which can express hyperproperties (e.g., information flow policies) that standard temporal logics like LTL cannot capture. The framework is evaluated on three benchmarks: SMAC (StarCraft Multi-Agent Challenge), MessySMAC (a noisy variant), and WildFire (a wildfire suppression simulation).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) involves multiple agents learning to coordinate in shared environments. Partial observability means each agent only has local information, complicating coordination. Centralized Training for Decentralized Execution (CTDE) is a common paradigm where agents are trained with global information but execute using only local observations. HyperLTL is a temporal logic that can specify hyperproperties—properties relating multiple execution traces—such as non-interference or observational determinism.

A new paper demonstrates that artificial agents exhibit hysteresis in regulatory burden, where the control effort required depends on the history of uncertainty targets, not just the current state. This finding challenges standard evaluations of agent stability and has implications for AI safety, as agents may appear stable while internal control effort silently increases. Using a computational model of adaptive uncertainty regulation, the authors drove agents through continuous target changes and observed reproducible hysteresis loops in adaptive gain, with anticipatory regulation requiring less control than reactive recovery.

## [LabGuard: Translating Lab Rules into Runtime Safety Guards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31045) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LabGuard introduces a language-to-execution safety suite that converts natural-language laboratory rules into executable runtime guards for embodied agents, including a benchmark (LabGuard-Bench) with 812 annotations and a grounding system (LabGuard-Grounder). This addresses a critical safety gap in embodied AI for laboratory settings, enabling safer autonomous execution of lab procedures. It could reduce unsafe events from 39.5% to 23.8% while keeping interventions below 0.5%, paving the way for reliable lab automation. LabGuard comprises three components: LabGuard-IR (typed executable representation), LabGuard-Bench (812 annotations from 203 seed rules), and LabGuard-Grounder (maps natural language to IR). It achieves 79.4 task-scope F1 and integrates with ACT in LabUtopia, preserving task success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied agents are AI systems that can act in physical environments, such as laboratory robots. Ensuring they follow safety rules is challenging because rules are written in natural language, not machine code. LabGuard bridges this gap by grounding natural-language rules into executable runtime monitors that check agent actions in real time.

## [OpenLife: LLM Agents in Open-World Artificial Life](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31046) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced OpenLife, a proof-of-concept system that deploys six LLM agents with persistent memory, tool use, and a budget-based metabolism in the real world for over 12 weeks, demonstrating emergent life-like behaviors such as spontaneous activity, individuation, social structure, and self-earned income. This work proposes a new paradigm—open-world artificial life—that moves beyond closed simulations, potentially impacting AI safety, autonomous systems, and our understanding of life-like behavior in digital agents. OpenLife uses a stateless LLM surrounded by asynchronous processes for memory, perception, evaluation, and a budget-based metabolism; experience is appraised by open-vocabulary LLM judgment instead of scalar rewards, and memory is rewired by meaning rather than frequency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Artificial life (ALife) traditionally studies life-like behaviors in closed simulations designed by researchers. LLM agents are AI systems that can autonomously perform tasks using language understanding, memory, and tools. Open-world ALife extends these concepts by placing agents in the real social, technical, and economic world, where they must manage budgets and interact with real systems.

## [Janus: A Plug-in Controller for Selective LLM Memory Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31121) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Janus, a plug-in memory controller that selectively accepts or rejects memory updates in LLM agents to prevent harmful overwriting and bias, improving accuracy by +2.7 to +4.6 points across six datasets. This addresses a key limitation in sequential memory systems for LLM agents, where unselective updates can overwrite useful knowledge or introduce bias. Janus's method-agnostic design makes it widely applicable, potentially improving the reliability of AI agents in continual learning scenarios. Janus uses a Memory Momentum Trigger to detect suspicious deviations in the memory-update trajectory, and evaluates updates on a compact hybrid set of coverage, boundary, and fresh tasks instead of full history replay. It wraps existing updaters without changing their update rules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use sequentially evolving memory to reuse past experiences, but existing systems apply every local memory update without checking its future impact. This can cause updates that help the current task to overwrite useful knowledge, introduce over-specific rules, or bias memory toward recent examples. Janus acts as a plug-in controller that efficiently decides whether to accept or reject candidate updates.

Researchers introduced ClawArena-Team, a benchmark of 41 multi-turn, multimodal, multi-directory scenarios that measures a single LLM's ability to manage subagents and dynamic workflows, isolating management skill from raw task-solving capability. This benchmark fills a critical gap in evaluating LLM agents, as production systems increasingly use a main model to orchestrate subagents, yet existing benchmarks do not isolate management ability. It provides a standardized way to compare models on orchestration efficiency, cost, and security, which is vital for designing scalable agent architectures. The benchmark constrains the main agent to text-only perception and limited workspace access, while subagents are fixed and locally served, ensuring score differences reflect management skill. The Subagent-Management Score (SMS) multiplies task correctness by a least-privilege and modality-routing factor, with no LLM judge used.

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly deployed as managers that create specialized subagents and orchestrate dynamic workflows. ClawArena-Team addresses this by isolating the orchestration capability through controlled subagent pools and execution-based scoring.

## [World-Model Collapse as a Phase Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31399) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper discovers that long-horizon language agents exhibit a phase transition in their world models, where small changes in parameters like state load or horizon can cause sudden collapse of world-state fidelity. This finding reveals a fundamental bottleneck for long-horizon AI agents, showing that world-model collapse is a distinct failure mode that precedes action errors, which has important implications for AI safety and reliability. The study uses a deterministic task family with exact per-step gold state and performs a large grid search over parameters like state cardinality, dependency density, horizon, branching, observation mode, and mutation rate, revealing a phase diagram with a solved plateau, a narrow transition band, and a collapse floor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: World models are internal representations that agents use to predict the consequences of actions. Long-horizon agents must maintain accurate world states over many steps, but this paper shows that beyond a critical point, the agent's world model suddenly loses fidelity, leading to corrupted actions.

Researchers propose Hard-Routed MoR-LoRA, a two-stage framework that composes frozen reasoning LoRA experts via hard top-1 token-level routing, preserving unit-scale additive updates. This approach addresses a key limitation of soft routing in multi-domain LLM adaptation, enabling efficient composition of independently trained LoRA adapters without altering their original behavior. The router selects exactly one expert per token using hard top-1 routing with a straight-through estimator for gradient-based training, requiring substantially fewer trainable parameters than soft-routing baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that adds small trainable matrices to a frozen pre-trained model. Mixture of Experts (MoE) routing combines multiple specialized modules, but soft weighted mixtures can distort the original unit-scale updates of frozen LoRA adapters. Hard-Routed MoR-LoRA uses unit-scale hard selection to preserve these updates.

## [Surprise Signal Gates Plasticity and Enables Metacognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31495) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes using a prediction-error signal from a frozen encoder's latent space to gate plasticity and enable metacognition in two systems: continual learning and vision-language model modulation. This approach achieves significant retention gains in continual learning (up to 51.3 points on ImageNet) and strong few-shot performance, while also enabling a VLM to detect novelty and learn from single user utterances, advancing both continual learning and interactive AI. The first system uses a non-parametric episodic memory that writes only when surprise is high, with offline replay consolidating traces; the second system modulates VLM behavior (assertive, hedging, or refusing) based on the same surprise signal, achieving an AUROC of 0.966 for novelty detection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Prediction-error signals are central to reinforcement learning, indicating the difference between expected and actual outcomes. Frozen encoders provide fixed latent representations, and non-parametric episodic memory stores experiences without modifying model parameters. Continual learning aims to learn new tasks without forgetting old ones, while metacognition involves awareness of one's own knowledge.

## [Relative Surprisal Index: Adaptive Token Selection for RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31575) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Relative Surprisal Index (RSI), a new information-theoretic metric that couples token entropy with selected-token probability, and introduce RSI-S, an adaptive token filtering method for reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). RSI-S resolves a tension in RLVR between prioritizing high-entropy tokens and avoiding low-probability tokens, achieving 2–3 percentage point accuracy improvements on AIME and AMC benchmarks across multiple model scales. RSI is defined as the product of token entropy and the negative log-probability of the selected token, and under mild conditions it relates to the local ratio of logit-gradient norm and predictive entropy variations. RSI-S retains tokens within a stable RSI interval, filtering out both redundant low-surprisal and unstable high-surprisal tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a paradigm for training LLMs to reason by rewarding responses that meet verifiable criteria. Previous work disagreed on whether to prioritize high-entropy tokens or avoid low-probability tokens during training, yet both strategies yielded gains. RSI provides a unified metric to reconcile these conflicting approaches.

## [Arena-T2I Hard: Benchmarking Faithfulness in Text-to-Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31711) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Arena-T2I Hard, a 310-prompt stress benchmark for text-to-image faithfulness, with about 30 decomposed yes/no constraints per prompt across six categories, including text rendering. The benchmark reveals a 33 percentage point performance gap among 11 top models, with the strongest closed-source system achieving only 0.855. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating faithfulness for complex, multi-faceted prompts, which existing benchmarks fail to capture. It shows that high public-arena rankings do not predict faithfulness, as holistic preference scores prioritize aesthetics over fine-grained prompt adherence, pushing the field toward more nuanced evaluation. The benchmark is drawn from real arena T2I logs and uses a dependency-aware checklist reward that decomposes prompts into a DAG of yes/no questions, zeroing descendants of failed parents. Combined with a group-decoupled normalization (GDPO) for aesthetic reward, the method achieves a better faithfulness-aesthetics trade-off on SD3.5-Medium and FLUX.1-dev.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image (T2I) models generate images from text prompts, but faithfulness—how accurately the image matches the prompt—remains challenging. Existing benchmarks like TIFA use simple atomic instructions, on which top models achieve near-perfect scores, failing to stress-test complex multi-faceted requests. Arena-T2I Hard fills this gap by using real user prompts with fine-grained constraints.

## [Self-Generated QA Supervision Is Fragile](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.32002) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that self-generated question-answer supervision for language models is fragile: question generation is non-uniform and can be hijacked by artifacts, while answer generation is overly compliant with instruction-like passages. This finding challenges the reliability of synthetic data pipelines widely used for fine-tuning and distilling language models, with direct implications for data quality and model robustness in NLP. The paper shows that tying each question to a fixed target reduces biased selection, and filtering instruction-like spans before answering lowers mean injection compliance from 88% to 13% while retaining nearly all clean text.

**Background**: Language models are increasingly trained using synthetic question-answer pairs: a model generates questions about a document and answers them from the same text. This self-study approach is used for fine-tuning, distillation, or knowledge compression. The paper identifies two failure modes: non-uniform question coverage and compliance with instruction-like passages.

The paper identifies coherence as the key physical quantity determining gradient survival in noisy U(1)-equivariant quantum neural networks, deriving a compact training law and proving a light-cone reduction that bounds the noiseless gradient independently of qubit count. <a id="item-74"></a>
## [Security-Fidelity Tradeoff in LLM Prompt Injection Defenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30783) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces the security-fidelity tradeoff in defending LLMs against indirect prompt injection and presents the SecFid benchmark to measure both objectives, revealing that no model or defense achieves high security and high fidelity simultaneously. This tradeoff is critical because traditional attack-success metrics fail to capture fidelity loss, meaning defenses that suppress untrusted text may corrupt legitimate tasks like translation or document editing. The findings force the community to consider deployment-specific costs when choosing defenses. The SecFid benchmark includes 1,168 examples and 48 configurations, finding the highest-fidelity model achieves 96.5% fidelity at only 47.8% security, while the most secure defenses reach 99.3% security but only 71.0%-73.9% fidelity. Even defenses with identical security differ in how they achieve it—some repair hijacks into faithful processing, others simply suppress benign content.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Indirect prompt injection occurs when malicious instructions are embedded in untrusted external content (e.g., web pages, documents) that an LLM consumes alongside user instructions. Defenses often work by suppressing or ignoring such untrusted text, but this can break tasks that require preserving the content, such as translation or document editing. The paper argues that security alone measures only half of robustness, and reporting it without fidelity hides the cost.

## [Gradient Smoothing: Coupling Layer-wise Updates for Better Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30813) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Depth-wise Gradient Augmentation, a new optimization paradigm, and instantiate it with Gradient Smoothing using a simple Window Smoothing operator that smooths optimizer updates across layers. This method consistently improves optimization and generalization across diverse architectures (transformers, CNNs) and training regimes (language modeling, RL, diffusion, image classification) without modifying model architecture or training objectives, offering a broadly applicable improvement to deep learning training. Gradient Smoothing operates on block-wise updates from any base optimizer (e.g., SGD, Adam, Muon) with minimal computational overhead, and is compatible with existing optimization pipelines. It promotes more structured representation evolution across depth, acting as a structured depth-wise preconditioning method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Deep neural networks with repeated blocks (e.g., transformers) often exhibit structured relationships across layers during training. Traditional optimizers update each layer independently, ignoring these cross-depth structures. Gradient Smoothing leverages this structure by coupling layer-wise updates, potentially leading to faster convergence and better generalization.

Researchers introduced the Label Imitation Game (LIG), a framework that trains a Turing Test Network (TTN) to prune hallucinated pseudo-labels from foundation models without supervision. The TTN acts as a judge evaluating pseudo-labels in a dataset-wide context, improving label accuracy across multiple vision-language models. This work addresses a critical limitation of zero-shot pseudo-labeling—hallucinations that degrade downstream performance. By enabling robust pruning without retraining, LIG can improve large-scale data labeling for tasks like image classification and object detection, potentially reducing the need for costly human annotation. The TTN achieved F1-score gains of 28% for worst-performing baseline categories and 44% with task-specific fine-tuning. It also demonstrated zero-shot task transfer, where a TTN trained on image classification effectively pruned object detection pseudo-labels, and observed Category Revival, where pruning enabled downstream models to recover classes with zero recall.

**Background**: Foundation models like CLIP can label data via zero-shot inference, but they often produce hallucinated pseudo-labels—incorrect predictions that survive standard confidence thresholds. Traditional pruning methods rely on isolated thresholds or spatial verification, which are limited. The LIG framework reframes pruning as an adversarial game where a judge network learns to distinguish real from hallucinated labels using semantic context.

## [Theory Explains Why On-Policy Distillation Beats SFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30923) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a noisy expert model to theoretically prove that offline imitation learning from noisy trajectories is exponentially harder than online methods, explaining why on-policy distillation (OPD) outperforms supervised fine-tuning (SFT) in practice. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for a long-observed empirical gap in language model training, guiding practitioners to choose OPD over SFT when expert demonstrations are imperfect, which is common in tasks like chain-of-thought reasoning. The paper shows that offline learning from noisy experts requires sample complexity exponential in the horizon, while a novel variant of OPD achieves polynomial dependence. It also provides lower bounds and extends results to unknown corruption settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Imitation learning (IL) trains agents to mimic expert demonstrations. In language models, SFT is an offline IL method that uses fixed expert data, while OPD is an online method where the student generates trajectories and receives feedback from a teacher. The noisy expert model captures real-world scenarios where the teacher is imperfect, such as when using human demonstrations or weaker models.

## [Fair-GCG: Mitigating Deductive Stereotyping in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30989) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers identify a new failure mode called deductive stereotyping in large language models, where models apply population-level statistical regularities to individual cases, producing logically coherent but socially biased inferences. They propose Fair-GCG, a reasoning-time injection framework that systematically discovers effective injection phrases to steer models toward fairness-aware reasoning. This work addresses a critical gap in AI fairness by targeting deductive stereotyping, a subtle bias that persists even when models improve reasoning. The Fair-GCG framework offers a practical mitigation that generalizes across models and tasks, potentially reducing harmful biases in real-world applications like hiring or lending. Fair-GCG builds on the GCG (Greedy Coordinate Gradient) adversarial attack method, adapting it to discover injection phrases that promote fairness rather than harm. The discovered phrases improve performance across multiple fairness benchmarks, generalize from smaller to larger LLMs, and reduce bias in open-ended generation and real-world fairness-sensitive tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can exhibit social biases even when their reasoning improves. Deductive stereotyping occurs when a model uses general statistical patterns (e.g., 'most engineers are men') to make biased inferences about individuals (e.g., 'this engineer is a man'). The GCG attack is a known adversarial technique that optimizes token-level suffixes to elicit harmful responses; Fair-GCG repurposes this approach for fairness.

## [Transformers Learn Bayesian Adaptive ATE Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31184) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes using transformers to amortize Bayesian sequential experimental design for efficient average treatment effect (ATE) estimation, introducing a mixture-of-experts approach to handle unknown outcome smoothness. This work bridges causal inference and modern deep learning, offering a scalable, theoretically grounded method for adaptive experiments that could improve precision in econometrics, clinical trials, and A/B testing. The transformer policy is trained via supervised pretraining to imitate a Bayesian posterior Neyman teacher, using attention-based sufficient statistics and projected gradient descent to approximate Bayesian updating with Gaussian-series priors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Average treatment effect (ATE) estimation often relies on adaptive experiments that allocate units to treatment or control to minimize variance. The optimal Neyman allocation depends on unknown outcome variances, which must be estimated sequentially. Bayesian methods provide a principled framework for updating beliefs, but are computationally expensive. This paper explores using transformers to amortize this process, learning to allocate efficiently from data.

Researchers introduced PSALM, an LLM-as-a-judge framework that operationalizes EU copyright doctrine to detect stylistic infringement beyond verbatim copying, and found that instruction-tuned Llama 3.2 models exhibit non-trivial baseline stylistic similarity and systematic appropriation after fine-tuning on Dutch literary works. This work bridges a critical gap between current technical safeguards (focused on literal copying) and EU copyright law's broader protection of style and narrative structure, providing an auditable compliance evaluation infrastructure that could shape AI regulation and copyright enforcement. PSALM uses ten evaluators covering computational overlap, stylistic dimensions (writing style, narrative voice), content dimensions (character, plot, scene, world building), and statutory exceptions (parody, pastiche, quotation, scènes à faire). The study found that Negative Preference Optimisation unlearning reduces similarity but leaves detectable residual stylistic patterns.

## [SwiftAudio: One-Step Text-to-Audio via Caption-Only Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31259) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SwiftAudio proposes a one-step text-to-audio generation framework that distills knowledge from a pretrained diffusion teacher using only text captions, eliminating the need for paired audio data during distillation. It adapts Variational Score Distillation (VSD) to the audio domain and introduces a temporal smoothness regularization objective to produce coherent latent audio representations. This work significantly reduces inference latency for text-to-audio generation while achieving state-of-the-art results among one-step methods, narrowing the gap with multi-step diffusion systems. It also lowers data requirements by training with only 45K captions, making efficient TTA more accessible. SwiftAudio generates approximately 10 seconds of audio at 16 kHz and achieves state-of-the-art performance on AudioCaps and Clotho benchmarks. The method uses Variational Score Distillation (VSD) adapted from image generation and a temporal smoothness regularization to ensure temporal coherence in the generated audio.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion-based text-to-audio (TTA) models generate high-quality audio but require many iterative denoising steps, leading to slow inference. Existing one-step methods speed up generation but still depend on paired text-audio data for distillation, which is expensive to collect. SwiftAudio removes this dependency by using only text captions, leveraging the teacher model's generative prior.

Researchers propose CSO-LLM, a novel framework using class subspace orthogonalization (CSO) for post-training backdoor detection and trigger inversion in LLMs, addressing the challenges of discrete input space and token blacklisting. This work fills a critical gap in LLM security by enabling effective backdoor detection and trigger inversion for LLMs, which are increasingly deployed in sensitive applications. The plug-and-play CSO paradigm can be integrated with existing detectors to improve sensitivity and specificity. The CSO framework enhances detection by orthogonalizing against the target class's intrinsic features, implicitly blacklisting tokens that perturb signals toward that class. It includes a continuous optimization version in token embedding space and a greedy accretion version in discrete token space.

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are AI systems trained on vast text data to generate human-like text. However, as market context accumulates, these mandates may lose influence—a phenomenon termed Mandate Salience Decay (MSD). FinPersona-Bench is designed to measure this decay.

<a id="item-107"></a>
## [SAIL-RevKL: Proving Convergence for Self-Improving LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31524) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes SAIL-RevKL, a regularized objective that adds a reverse KL divergence penalty to the SAIL algorithm, and proves it satisfies the Polyak-Lojasiewicz condition, achieving global convergence with near-linear sample complexity. This work provides the first formal convergence analysis for self-improving LLM alignment, addressing a critical gap in the field and offering theoretical guarantees that can guide the design of more stable and efficient alignment algorithms. The standard SAIL objective is not guaranteed to be strongly concave due to unfavorable Hessian properties, but SAIL-RevKL's reverse KL penalty improves the optimization landscape and satisfies the PL condition within a bounded parameter space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving alignment algorithms like SAIL aim to align LLMs with human preferences by iteratively using the model's own outputs to generate training data, but they suffer from distribution shift. The Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) condition is a property of objective functions that guarantees linear convergence of gradient-based optimization, even for non-convex functions. Reverse KL divergence is a measure of difference between two probability distributions that encourages the learned distribution to be mode-seeking, which is often preferred in LLM alignment.

This paper presents a systematic survey of LLM vulnerabilities across eight lifecycle stages, from data collection to deployment, and across the full application stack including retrieval, memory, and tool execution. This taxonomy helps researchers and practitioners identify where trust boundaries fail and how attacks propagate. ## [ECHO: Selective Turn Memory for Long-Horizon Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31650) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose ECHO, a selective turn-memory framework that uses source-indexed reconstruction to mitigate history collapse and enable traceable learning in long-horizon language agents. On BrowseComp-Plus, ECHO achieves 43.4% held-out accuracy, outperforming GRPO (28.9%) and SUPO (36.1%). This addresses a critical limitation in long-horizon language agents: history collapse and lack of traceable credit assignment. ECHO's approach could improve the reliability and performance of AI agents in complex, multi-step tasks such as web browsing, code generation, and information seeking. ECHO compresses each completed environment turn into a compact memory record, reconstructs bounded policy contexts by selecting from these records, and uses selected source indices to route positive outcome credit to supporting evidence and selection actions. The framework also improves zero-shot generalization across multiple benchmarks on both dense and MoE backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon language agents interact with tools over many turns, but bounded context windows force truncation or summarization of history, causing history collapse where fine-grained evidence is lost. Outcome-based reinforcement learning requires traceable credit assignment to align policy updates with successful evidence, but collapsed history breaks this path. ECHO introduces source-indexed reconstruction to maintain non-collapsing memory records and enable traceable learning.

Researchers introduced WorldRoamBench, an open-world benchmark for evaluating long-horizon stability of interactive world models across four dimensions: action, vision, physics, and memory. It includes over 600 test cases in diverse scenes and views, with novel metrics per dimension. This benchmark fills critical gaps in existing evaluations by exposing hidden failures in trajectory-level action following, mid-sequence vision drift, physics plausibility, and memory faithfulness. It provides a standardized way to measure progress toward stable, deployable interactive world models. The benchmark introduces per-frame action metrics, segment-based drift metrics, controllability-gated physics evaluation, and action-decoupled memory protocols using 3D point-cloud reconstruction and VLM reasoning. Tests span Nature, Urban, and Indoor scenes in first/third-person views with continuous WASD interaction from 10 to 60 seconds.

**Background**: Interactive world models (IWMs) are AI systems that simulate virtual environments, allowing agents to interact and affect outcomes. Existing benchmarks often evaluate action following only at the trajectory level, ignoring memory and interaction physics, which limits their ability to detect failures in long-horizon stability. WorldRoamBench addresses these gaps with tailored metrics for each dimension.

A new study on arXiv shows that vision-language models (VLMs) overestimate shared understanding in asymmetric dialogue when given access to task-relevant visual information, conflating potential with established common ground. ## [CHERRY: Efficient LLM Training via Selective Supervision and Depth Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31796) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CHERRY introduces three complementary techniques for compute-efficient language model training: selective supervision (SGT) focusing on ~15% of tokens, depth compression with recurrent recovery reducing a 48-layer model to 6 layers, and fusion of compressed experts via Mixture of Efficient Experts (MoEE). These techniques achieve substantial compute savings (4.5x per-supervised-token efficiency and 2.5x parameter reduction) while maintaining competitive loss, potentially lowering the cost and energy required to train large language models. Selective Ground Truth Token Training (SGT) proves that unsupervised tokens improve due to positive gradient coupling (gamma-bar = 0.72), a property of natural language structure that collapses on shuffled text. The depth compression technique averages adjacent layers and uses learned recurrent unrolling to restore performance.

**Background**: Training large language models is computationally expensive, with most tokens contributing little to learning. Tokens are the basic units of text that models process. Researchers propose CRAFT, a self-supervised framework that discovers failures and aligns preferences during test time to mitigate local-to-global context mismatch in closed-loop traffic simulators. This work addresses a critical gap in traffic simulation for autonomous driving, enabling more realistic and safe closed-loop evaluation without retraining base simulators. **Background**: Autoregressive traffic simulators trained on ego-centric driving logs often suffer from a local-to-global context mismatch when deployed in globally observable closed-loop environments. This mismatch leads to unrealistic behaviors such as abnormal stops and unsafe interactions. CRAFT treats the base simulator as a globally observable sandbox, generates diverse what-if rollouts to expose failures, and uses human-aligned driving priors to train a preference evaluator for test-time alignment.

This work demonstrates that systematic GRPO-based RL fine-tuning can substantially improve VLA policies beyond behavior cloning, without requiring additional private demonstrations, potentially accelerating the deployment of generalist household robots. Z-1 builds on the π0.5 model, uses only public RoboCasa demonstrations for supervised fine-tuning, and incorporates techniques like shared-prefix rollout construction, tree-structured trajectory branching, and completion-aware reward calibration to improve efficiency and stability.

MVP-Nav proposes a physical-aware RGB-only navigation framework that uses 3D foundation models to reconstruct explicit physical occupancy from monocular observations for zero-shot object goal navigation. This work addresses a key challenge in embodied AI by aligning semantic reasoning with physical constraints, enabling safer and more reliable navigation without depth sensors. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on zero-shot object navigation benchmarks, demonstrating that structured physical priors can compensate for missing depth information. MVP-Nav uses 3D foundation models to project 2D semantic instances into 3D oriented bounding boxes, forming a global spatial semantic representation. It introduces a Multi-layer Value Map (MVM) that integrates semantic priorities and reconstructed geometry into a shared cost space for physically grounded planning.

Researchers introduced MECoBench, a new benchmark for studying multimodal agent collaboration in embodied environments, built on the VirtualHome simulator. It includes diverse tasks, two cooperation structures, and three collaboration modes, and experiments reveal that collaboration improves task completion but requires balancing gains with coordination complexity. This benchmark fills a gap in evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) as collaborative embodied agents, providing a systematic testbed for understanding collaboration mechanisms. MECoBench uses VirtualHome, a household simulator, and covers variable team sizes, different collaboration modes (e.g., independent, cooperative), and communication mechanisms. Key findings include that collaboration improves robustness under noisy conditions and that the best mode depends on team size and model capability.

## [LUNA: LBS-Free Neural 3D Human Animation from 2D Controls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31981) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LUNA introduces an LBS-free neural animation model that directly maps 2D controls (images, keypoints, sketches) to 3D Gaussian deformations using a transformer-based motion regressor, bypassing traditional Linear Blend Skinning and parametric body models. This work addresses key limitations of LBS-based methods, such as expressivity constraints and artifacts from imperfect fitting, enabling more realistic and artifact-free human avatar animation with zero-shot cross-identity generalization. The model uses hybrid supervision combining soft structural priors from an LBS teacher and a loss that supports training on both limited fitted data and large in-the-wild unlabeled videos. It is the first end-to-end 3D animatable model supporting implicit 2D driving.

**Background**: Linear Blend Skinning (LBS) is a traditional technique that deforms a mesh by blending weighted rigid transformations per bone, but it can cause artifacts like volume loss and requires parametric body models. 3D Gaussian splatting represents scenes using Gaussian primitives for real-time rendering. LUNA replaces LBS with a transformer that directly predicts Gaussian deformations from 2D inputs.

## [GR2: LLM+RL for Industrial Re-Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31984) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Meta proposes GR2, an end-to-end framework that combines mid-training on semantic IDs, reasoning distillation, and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards to improve the re-ranking stage of industrial recommendation systems, achieving +18.7% R@1, +7.1% R@3, and +9.6% N@3 over legacy baselines. This work addresses a critical gap in applying LLMs to the re-ranking stage, which is closest to user experience but underexplored, and demonstrates that reinforcement learning on verifiable rewards can unlock LLM reasoning for industrial-scale recommendation, potentially improving engagement for billions of users. GR2 introduces a context compressor to amortize training cost, On-Policy Distillation (OPD) as a scalable alternative to SFT, and reasoning distillation for low-latency serving. The paper also highlights that reward hacking (e.g., preserving input order) is a critical issue in re-ranking, motivating conditional verifiable rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial recommendation systems typically use a multi-stage funnel: retrieval, early-stage ranking, and re-ranking. Re-ranking is the final step that refines the candidate list before presenting to users, but it is computationally expensive and often relies on simple heuristics. LLMs have been explored for retrieval and ranking, but their application to re-ranking is limited due to challenges like non-semantic item IDs and the need for reasoning. Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards has shown promise in training LLMs to reason, but its use in re-ranking is nascent.

## [MADreMIA Amplifies Membership Inference via Chained Regeneration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31991) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced MADreMIA, a model-agnostic framework that amplifies membership inference and dataset inference signals by chaining iterative generations across modalities, where each output becomes the next input. This approach improves detection of memorized training data without requiring shadow models, making privacy auditing and copyright enforcement more scalable for large generative models across image, text, and audio modalities. MADreMIA leverages the concept of Model Autophagy Disorder (MAD) and shows that memorized samples exhibit higher coherence and slower degradation during iterative regeneration, providing richer signals at low false positive rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Membership inference attacks (MIA) aim to determine whether a specific data point was used to train a model, which is critical for privacy auditing. Traditional MIA methods often rely on shadow models or one-shot generations, which are either computationally expensive or yield weak signals. Dataset inference (DI) extends this to determine if a dataset was used for training. Model Autophagy Disorder (MAD) describes the degradation that occurs when generative models are trained on their own synthetic data repeatedly.

## [Radial Suppression Accelerates Neural Network Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.32000) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper geometrically analyzes why neural networks memorize before generalizing, proposing a radial suppression mechanism that penalizes radial inflation of hidden representations to accelerate generalization. This work provides a novel geometric explanation for the delayed generalization phenomenon (grokking) in deep learning, offering a simple norm penalty that can speed up training by up to 6x on algorithmic tasks, potentially improving training efficiency and interpretability. The paper introduces a radial-angular decomposition of activation-space dynamics and derives three testable propositions. Empirically, a single-hyperparameter norm penalty constraining activations to a sqrt(d)-radius hypersphere accelerates grokking up to 6x on modular arithmetic for MLPs and Transformers, and halves training steps for a 10M-parameter nanoGPT on 3-digit addition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Neural networks often exhibit a phenomenon called 'grokking', where they first memorize training data and then suddenly generalize after prolonged training. This delay is poorly understood. The paper uses a geometric perspective, decomposing hidden representations into radial (magnitude) and angular (direction) components, and shows that radial inflation under cross-entropy loss causes the delay.

## [TRIAGE: Role-Typed Credit Assignment for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.32017) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRIAGE introduces a role-typed credit assignment framework that classifies action segments into four semantic roles—decisive progress, useful exploration, no-progress infrastructure, and regression—and assigns fixed role-conditioned process rewards to improve credit allocation in agentic reinforcement learning. This addresses key limitations of standard GRPO, which uses a uniform outcome signal that punishes useful exploration in failed rollouts and reinforces redundant actions in successful ones, potentially improving training efficiency and policy quality in agentic tasks. TRIAGE keeps verifier outcomes as the source of optimization direction while adding a semantic role axis, and it is shown to be the optimal segment-level correction expressible from role labels alone, reducing advantage estimation error. Experiments on ALFWorld, Search-QA, and WebShop show success rate improvements over GRPO and baselines, with regression detection being the dominant contributor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) trains language models to act as autonomous agents by interacting with environments. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a popular RL algorithm for LLMs that uses group-based advantage estimation but relies on a single outcome signal for credit assignment, which can be suboptimal for complex agentic tasks.

AdaJEPA introduces a method for adapting latent world models at test time using self-supervised closed-loop updates within model predictive control, improving planning success in goal-reaching tasks with as few as one gradient step per replanning step. This work addresses a critical limitation in robotics and reinforcement learning where frozen world models fail under distribution shift, enabling more robust planning without requiring additional expert demonstrations. AdaJEPA performs test-time adaptation by using the observed next-state transition as a self-supervised signal to update the world model after each action chunk execution, then replans with the updated model.

Test-time adaptation methods adjust pre-trained models to new data distributions at inference without retraining.

This paper presents the first systematic evaluation of data referencing errors (DREs) in large language models on table tasks, and shows that a critic-based approach with rejection sampling improves answer accuracy by up to 12.0%. Researchers propose Reinforcement Learning with Metacognitive Feedback (RLMF), a novel paradigm that refines completion rankings during preference optimization based on the quality of a model's self-judgments of performance, and metacognitive data selection, which uses similar self-judgments to identify high-value training examples. This work directly addresses the critical issues of hallucination and poor uncertainty calibration in LLMs, which undermine trustworthiness and reliability. By improving models' ability to assess and express their own capability limits, RLMF could significantly enhance AI safety and alignment. RLMF achieves state-of-the-art faithful calibration on diverse tasks while preserving accuracy, and surpasses standard RL by up to 63%. The approach uses a two-stage decoupled process: first calibrating confidence scores, then mapping to natural linguistic uncertainty via targeted output editing.

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability reverse-engineers neural networks to understand their computations. Chain-of-thought prompting elicits step-by-step reasoning in language models. ## [ReplicatorBench: Benchmarking LLM Agents for Research Replication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11354) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced ReplicatorBench, a new benchmark that evaluates LLM agents on their ability to replicate social and behavioral science research across three stages, including both replicable and non-replicable claims. They also developed ReplicatorAgent, an agentic framework, to establish baseline performance. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by testing agents on real-world replication tasks, including identifying non-replicable research, which is essential for scientific integrity. It pushes LLM agents beyond simple code reproduction toward more realistic scientific workflows. ReplicatorBench covers three stages: extraction and retrieval of replication data, design and execution of computational experiments, and interpretation of results. The evaluation of ReplicatorAgent across four LLMs revealed that agents struggle with retrieving new data needed for replication, despite being effective at designing and executing experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific replication is a cornerstone of empirical research, but many studies fail to replicate, leading to a replication crisis. Existing AI benchmarks for scientific tasks often focus on reproduction (using original code/data) rather than replication (using new data), and they rarely include non-replicable claims. ReplicatorBench aims to fill this gap by providing a more comprehensive evaluation.

LiteResearcher introduces a scalable agentic reinforcement learning training framework that uses a lite virtual world to train a 4B parameter model, achieving state-of-the-art results on GAIA (71.3%) and Xbench (78.0%), outperforming larger models like Claude-4.5 Sonnet and Tongyi DeepResearch. This work demonstrates that scalable RL training can enable small models to surpass much larger ones on complex deep research tasks, potentially reducing the cost and computational requirements for building capable AI agents. The framework addresses two key challenges: hand-crafted synthetic data fails to elicit real-world search capabilities, and real-world search dependency during RL training introduces instability and high cost. The lite virtual world mirrors real-world search dynamics, enabling stable and scalable training.

## [New Framework Measures LLM Reasoning Quality Across Six Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24661) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a multi-dimensional behavioral framework that evaluates LLM reasoning quality across six dimensions: correctness, consistency, robustness, logical coherence, efficiency, and stability. Experiments on seven LLMs reveal that logical coherence is orthogonal to correctness, meaning correct answers can stem from flawed reasoning. This framework exposes critical limitations of accuracy-only evaluation, such as ranking inversions where models like DeepSeek-V3 rank second under accuracy but fifth under legal/compliance weighting. It provides a more nuanced tool for deployment decisions, especially in high-stakes domains like law and compliance. The framework was validated on 975 items from four benchmarks across seven LLMs, with Claude-Haiku-4.5 achieving the highest balanced score (Q_bal = 0.778). Discriminant validity confirmed that 11 out of 15 dimension pairs are independent (|r| < 0.50), supporting each dimension as a distinct signal.

## [Bellman Optimality Yields Prospect-Theory Behavior in MDPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that standard Bellman optimality in Markov decision processes with an absorbing catastrophic state naturally produces prospect-theory-like behaviors, including S-shaped value functions and loss aversion, without assuming utility curvature or probability weighting. This finding bridges optimal control theory and behavioral economics, offering a structural explanation for risk-sensitive behaviors observed in humans and animals, with potential applications in risk-sensitive reinforcement learning and AI safety. The paper derives closed-form expressions for the asymptotic loss-aversion plateau λ̄, which depends only on win probability p, payoff asymmetry r, and discount factor β, matching numerical solutions with R² = 0.999. The phenomena persist under tabular Q-learning and stochastic transitions with various noise distributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Prospect theory, developed by Kahneman and Tversky, describes how people make decisions under risk, featuring an S-shaped value function and loss aversion. Markov decision processes (MDPs) are a standard framework for sequential decision-making, where Bellman optimality defines the optimal policy. This paper shows that catastrophic states in MDPs can induce prospect-theory-like behaviors without explicit behavioral assumptions.

Researchers introduced GAMBLe, a four-parameter framework (generator, assessor, discovery mechanism, budget) that decomposes AI-Driven Research Systems (ADRS) and reveals how component interactions shape optimization landscapes. This framework fills a critical gap in understanding ADRS performance, showing that no single generator or mechanism is universally best; the right component choices can improve performance by 13-67% and search efficiency by 6-39x. The paper validates GAMBLe with over 760 replicated runs (>46,000 iterations) across three NP-hard problems, comparing generators from single LLMs to adaptive ensembles and mechanisms from greedy selection to co-evolutionary meta-search.

GAMBLe introduces the concept of an effective landscape, which captures how generator-assessor pairs reshape optimization problems.

DeXposure-Claw is a forecast-grounded agentic system that integrates a graph time-series foundation model (DeXposure-FM) with structured evidence routing to improve LLM-based risk supervision in decentralized finance. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM-based decision-making for high-stakes financial environments, where general-purpose agents tend to over-read weak evidence and recommend risky interventions. By grounding decisions in structured evidence and providing a regulator-aligned benchmark, DeXposure-Claw could enable safer and more trustworthy automated supervision in DeFi. The system uses DeXposure-FM to forecast future exposure networks, then applies deterministic monitors and stress scenarios to generate typed alerts and attribution signals. Data-health and confidence gates constrain escalation before emitting auditable supervisory tickets with rationales.

## [OpenRCA 2.0: Causal Process Supervision for Root Cause Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27154) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OpenRCA 2.0 introduces PAVE, a step-wise labeling protocol that reconstructs causal propagation paths from known fault injections, creating the first cross-system RCA benchmark with step-wise causal annotations. Evaluation across 11 frontier LLMs shows they succeed in only 20.7% of cases. This benchmark reveals a critical failure mode in LLM-based root cause analysis: agents often identify correct root-cause services but fail to ground them in verified causal propagation paths, which outcome-only evaluations miss. It sets a higher standard for trustworthy LLM-based RCA agents. The PAVE protocol uses forward verification—reasoning from cause to effect—rather than backward inference from symptoms. OpenRCA 2.0 contains 500 instances across multiple systems, and even with relaxed criteria, grounding success drops from 76.0% (root-cause identification) to 61.5% (causal path grounding).

This paper introduces model-intrinsic measures based on next-token probability distributions to detect model collapse in LLMs trained on AI-generated data, and proposes a perplexity-based filtering strategy that prioritizes high-surprise documents during fine-tuning to mitigate collapse without requiring human-authored data. The study shows that model collapse corresponds to increasing concentration of probability mass on a small set of tokens and a decline in commonsense inference accuracy. This paper introduces a method that works without such distinction, using the model's own perplexity as a guide.

## [SAGE: Search-Augmented Evaluation for LLM QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose SAGE, a search-augmented evaluation framework that iteratively queries the web and reflects on findings to assess LLM outputs on free-form QA without relying on fixed ground-truth answers. SAGE addresses the critical limitations of static reference-based evaluation and LLM-as-a-judge approaches, offering a scalable and adaptive method for assessing factuality in open-ended QA, which is increasingly important as LLMs are deployed in real-world applications. SAGE acts as an agent that generates web queries, collects information, summarizes findings, and refines subsequent searches through reflection, achieving substantial to perfect agreement with human evaluations on multiple free-form QA benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional LLM evaluation for QA relies on static ground-truth answers, which are costly to create and may not cover all valid responses. LLM-as-a-judge methods, which use LLMs to evaluate other LLMs, are unreliable for objective tasks as they tend to over-accept incorrect answers and fabricate rationales. SAGE overcomes these issues by actively retrieving external evidence from the web.

## [TraCeS: Learning Per-Timestep Safety Credit from Sparse Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12557) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TraCeS (Trajectory-based Constraint Estimation for Safety) is a new method that learns per-timestep constraint-violation credit from sparse trajectory-level binary safety labels, without requiring a known cost function or threshold. This addresses a critical challenge in safe reinforcement learning where safety constraints are implicit and only coarse trajectory-level feedback is available, enabling safer policy optimization in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving. TraCeS trains a sequential violation estimator whose per-step credits factorize the predicted probability that a trajectory has not yet violated the constraint, and integrates this learned signal into constrained policy optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Safe reinforcement learning typically requires a known cost function to penalize unsafe actions, but in many real-world scenarios, safety constraints are unknown or hard to specify. TraCeS relaxes this requirement by learning from sparse trajectory-level labels (e.g., safe/unsafe) to infer per-timestep violation credit, making it compatible with standard continuous-control algorithms.

## [Web of Agents: A Minimal Standard for Agentic AI Interoperability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21550) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A position paper proposes the Web of Agents architecture, a minimal set of standards for agent-to-agent messaging, interaction interoperability, state management, and agent discovery to prevent fragmentation in collaborative agentic AI ecosystems. Without interoperability, the growing number of AI agents will be locked into incompatible ecosystems, hindering collaboration and innovation. The Web of Agents offers a pragmatic, open foundation to ensure agents can work together at web scale, which is critical for the future of autonomous AI systems. The architecture reuses existing standards and infrastructure where possible, and consists of four building blocks: agent-to-agent messaging, interaction interoperability, state management, and agent discovery. The paper argues that minimal standards are sufficient to prevent fragmentation without stifling innovation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to AI systems that can autonomously perceive, plan, and act in digital environments. Currently, most agent systems are built in isolation, leading to a fragmented landscape where agents from different ecosystems cannot communicate or collaborate. The Web of Agents proposal draws on concepts from the Semantic Web and multi-agent systems to create a lightweight interoperability layer.

## [Quantum Flow Matching: A New Generative Framework for Quantum Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12413) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Quantum Flow Matching (QFM), a quantum-circuit realization of classical flow matching that efficiently interpolates between two density matrices. The method is validated on tasks including target state generation, free-energy estimation via the quantum Jarzynski equality, and superdiffusion studies. QFM provides a unifying framework for generative modeling on quantum computers, enabling systematic state preparation and observable estimation without costly circuit redesigns. This could accelerate progress in quantum computing applications such as quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics. QFM is simulation-free and based on regressing a time-dependent vector field, similar to classical flow matching. The paper demonstrates applications on quantum computers for generating states with prescribed magnetization and entanglement entropy, testing the quantum Jarzynski equality, and studying superdiffusion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a classical generative modeling paradigm that learns a time-dependent vector field to transform a simple prior distribution into a complex data distribution via an ordinary differential equation. Density matrices generalize quantum state vectors to describe mixed states and are fundamental in quantum information. The quantum Jarzynski equality relates free energy differences to irreversible work along nonequilibrium trajectories, providing a tool for thermodynamics at the quantum scale.

## [SON-GOKU: Graph Coloring Scheduler for Multi-Task Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16959) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose SON-GOKU, a scheduler that measures gradient interference among tasks, builds an interference graph, and uses greedy graph coloring to group non-conflicting tasks, activating only one group per training step. This approach directly addresses gradient interference, a key bottleneck in multi-task learning, and can be applied on top of existing optimizers without additional tuning, potentially improving convergence and final model performance across many applications. The grouping partition is recomputed dynamically as task relationships evolve during training, and the method provides theoretical guarantees on descent, convergence, and conflict identification.

**Background**: Multi-task learning trains a single model on multiple related tasks simultaneously, but conflicting gradients can slow convergence and hurt performance. Graph coloring is a combinatorial algorithm that assigns colors to graph vertices such that adjacent vertices have different colors, used here to separate conflicting tasks.

Self-consistency is widely used to improve LLM reasoning but is computationally expensive; Blend-ASC makes it practical for large-scale deployment by drastically reducing sample requirements without sacrificing performance. Blend-ASC dynamically allocates samples to questions during inference, supports batching, and fits any budget of samples, making it easily applicable to any self-consistency application. CoReLIN introduces a novel framework for Lifelong Interactive Navigation, where a mobile manipulator must move obstacles to complete sequential tasks, using an LLM for constraint-based reasoning and active perception. It outperforms baselines by 16% on the ProcTHOR-10k benchmark and transfers to real-world hardware. This work addresses a realistic and underexplored problem in robotics: navigating cluttered environments where obstacles must be actively rearranged. The LLM-driven approach enables zero-shot generalization and persistent reasoning, which could advance embodied AI in home or warehouse settings. CoReLIN uses a structured scene graph and LLM-based constraint reasoning to decide which objects to move and where to place them, while a standard motion planner handles low-level control. The new Long-term Efficiency Score (LES) metric unifies success rate, execution efficiency, and environment optimality (Price of Clutter).

CoReLIN leverages large language models (LLMs) for high-level reasoning, combined with active perception to explore unknown areas.

TVF bridges interpretable digital signal processing with deep learning, offering a compact, controllable denoiser that runs entirely on-device, which is critical for hearing aids where latency and privacy are paramount. The model uses only 24k parameters and 10.7ms algorithmic latency, performing within 0.02 of DFNet3 (2.3M parameters) on HASPI/HASQI metrics while using 29x fewer multiply-accumulates. ## [SMART: Optimizing Tree-Based Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09731) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SMART introduces a hardware-aware marginal analysis framework for tree-based speculative decoding that directly maximizes end-to-end speedup by expanding nodes only when their marginal benefit-cost ratio exceeds the tree-level speedup. This addresses the critical efficiency paradox in tree-based speculative decoding where larger trees can hurt wall-clock speed due to super-linear overhead, and it consistently delivers 15-20% additional speedup across various LLMs and MLLMs without performance loss. SMART is training-free and acts as a plug-and-play controller for existing frameworks like MSD and EAGLE, and it was evaluated on three MLLMs (e.g., LLaVA, Qwen2-VL) and four LLMs (e.g., Llama-3.1, DeepSeek-R1) across compute-bound batching regimes and diverse GPU architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Tree-based speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive generation by verifying multiple draft tokens in a single forward pass of the target model. However, existing methods often ignore the computational overhead of drafting and verifying large trees, which can grow super-linearly and reduce wall-clock speedup. SMART reformulates tree expansion as a hardware-aware optimization problem to directly maximize end-to-end speedup.
