## [New Q-Learning Convergence Analysis via Switching Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19569) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a switching linear system (SLS) representation for Q-learning error dynamics and derives convergence rates using the joint spectral radius (JSR), which can be sharper than traditional row-sum bounds. This provides a novel theoretical framework for Q-learning analysis that may yield tighter convergence guarantees, potentially impacting the design and understanding of reinforcement learning algorithms. The analysis is the first to express the leading exponential rate of standard Q-learning via the JSR of the corresponding SLS model, and the resulting rate is tied to the intrinsic worst-case exponential rate of the direct SLS representation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Q-learning is a fundamental reinforcement learning algorithm that learns optimal action-value functions. Traditional convergence analyses often rely on row-sum contraction bounds, which can be conservative. Switching linear systems combine multiple linear dynamics with a switching rule, and the joint spectral radius generalizes the spectral radius to sets of matrices, quantifying the worst-case growth rate of products of matrices.

Researchers discovered that LLMs use shared task-specific attention heads, called lexical task heads, across instruction-based and example-based prompts, and that behavioral variability between prompts is explained by the degree of activation of these heads. This finding provides a mechanistic explanation for prompt sensitivity, a major practical issue with LLMs, and could lead to more robust prompt engineering and model improvements. Activation patching experiments showed that modulating lexical task head activations recovers between 10% and 90% of task performance variance, and failures sometimes arise from competing task representations diluting the target signal.

This paper identifies a subset of attention heads that literally encode the task description in their outputs, termed lexical task heads.

## [MeanFlow Instability Explained and Fixed via Optimal Control Variate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09235) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies that MeanFlow training instability is caused by a misused control variate coefficient in the Jacobi-vector product term, and derives the optimal coefficient in closed form, connecting to concurrent fixes. This provides a theoretical foundation for stabilizing one-step generative model training, which is critical for efficient inference in diffusion and flow-matching models, and unifies several concurrent practical fixes. The optimal coefficient is derived analytically, and experiments on 2D benchmarks and a latent Diffusion Transformer show that the gradient-MSE is minimized near β=0.94, but the FID-optimal coefficient prefers the unbiased corner (β=1).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: MeanFlow is a distillation-free method for one-step generative modeling that directly regresses to the time-averaged velocity field. However, its training is notoriously unstable with non-decreasing loss and unbounded gradient variance. The loss involves a conditional velocity field that serves both as a regression target and as a control variate in a Jacobi-vector product, and the original MeanFlow assigns the wrong coefficient to the latter role.

## [KV-RM Regularizes KV-Cache Movement for Static-Graph LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09735) ⭐️ 8.0/10

KV-RM is a runtime design that decouples logical KV histories from physical storage and uses a block pager and merge-staged transport to regularize KV-cache movement, enabling efficient static-graph LLM serving despite irregular request patterns. This addresses a critical performance bottleneck in LLM serving—irregular KV-cache behavior—by absorbing variability below a fixed decode interface, improving throughput and tail latency while reducing memory reservation. On a 2-GPU NVIDIA A100 node, KV-RM improves mixed-length decoding throughput and tail latency relative to a static-graph baseline, reduces reserved KV memory across workload families, and eliminates severe burst-time latency spikes under production-trace replay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: LLM token generation is memory-bandwidth bound, not compute bound; each step reads the whole KV cache from GPU memory. Static-graph decoders provide predictable launches but suffer from irregular KV-cache behavior due to varying request lengths and asynchronous EOS events, while dynamic runtimes like vLLM use paged KV management for flexibility but introduce overhead.

## [ChainCaps: Preventing Permission Laundering in AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26542) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ChainCaps introduces a capability budget system that prevents permission laundering in tool-using AI agents by ensuring that authority can only be preserved or reduced, never increased, as data flows through tool chains. This addresses a critical safety gap in open-ended AI agents, where composing multiple tools can lead to unsafe outcomes even if each tool individually passes permission checks. ChainCaps reduces attack success rates from up to 68% to below 5% while maintaining high benign task completion. ChainCaps is implemented as a transparent MCP proxy that requires no changes to the agent or tool servers. It uses sink-specific capability budgets and propagates them by intersection, ensuring monotonic capability attenuation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents can chain together multiple tools like file readers, code interpreters, and web APIs to accomplish complex tasks. Permission laundering occurs when an agent uses legitimate per-tool permissions in combination to produce an unauthorized end-to-end effect, such as reading a confidential document and sending it externally.

A new research paper shows that using typed knowledge graphs as a grounding substrate improves GPT-4 agent accuracy on industrial asset operations from 65% to 99% on graph-answerable scenarios, and introduces Generation-Augmented Knowledge (GAK) to handle missing data. This demonstrates that the data layer, not LLM orchestration, is the primary lever for accuracy in structured operational domains, potentially transforming how industrial AI systems are designed. The paper uses Cypher queries for structured retrieval and native graph primitives for deterministic execution, achieving 82-83% with LLM-generated Cypher and 99% with no LLM on graph-answerable scenarios. GAK lifts answerability from zero to 100% for equipment types absent from the graph.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often struggle with accuracy when reasoning over flat document stores. Knowledge graphs structure data as nodes and relationships, enabling precise querying. Cypher is a declarative graph query language used to retrieve data from property graphs. Generation-Augmented Knowledge (GAK) is a technique that materializes missing facts as provenance-tagged graph nodes.

## [INFUSER: Influence-Guided Co-Training Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09052) ⭐️ 8.0/10

INFUSER introduces an iterative co-training framework where a generator and solver co-evolve, using an influence score to reward the generator for questions that improve solver performance on the target distribution. On Qwen3-8B-Base, it achieves over 20% relative improvement on Olympiad and SuperGPQA benchmarks compared to strong self-evolution baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in self-evolution methods by replacing simple difficulty heuristics with an influence-based reward that directly measures a question's utility for solver improvement. It demonstrates that a smaller 8B model with co-evolving generator can outperform a frozen 32B thinking generator on math and coding, suggesting a more efficient path to scaling reasoning capabilities. The framework uses a dual-normalized variant of GRPO called DuGRPO to handle the continuous, noisy influence score for generator training. Ablations confirm each design choice is necessary, and extensions to instruction-finetuned anchors and rule-verifiable RLVR data demonstrate flexibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolution aims to improve a pretrained language model with minimal external supervision by having it generate its own training data. Existing methods often rely on curated data or simple difficulty heuristics that may not align with solver improvement. INFUSER introduces an influence score that measures how much a generated question helps the solver on the target distribution, creating an adaptive curriculum from unstructured documents.

## [SGCD: Better Credit Assignment for Long-Horizon Tool-Use RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12634) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Sibling-Guided Credit Distillation (SGCD), a method that uses contrastive sibling rollouts and an LLM summary to refine credit assignment in long-horizon tool-use reinforcement learning, outperforming GRPO on AppWorld and tau^3-airline benchmarks. SGCD addresses a key limitation in self-distillation for long-horizon tasks, where naive distillation can amplify harmful behaviors. By keeping policy gradient in charge and using distillation only for credit weighting, it achieves higher performance without altering the deployment policy. SGCD dynamically samples mixed successful and failed sibling rollouts, uses an external LLM to summarize their contrast into a stepwise credit reference, and reshapes GRPO token advantages via detached teacher/student divergence. On AppWorld, test_normal TGC improved from 42.9 to 45.6; on tau^3-airline, held-out evaluator score rose from 0.583 to 0.602.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon tool-use reinforcement learning learns from outcome verification, but trajectory-level advantages are broadcast over all tokens, making credit assignment sparse. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a popular RL algorithm that compares actions within a group to stabilize updates, but it still suffers from sparse rewards in long-horizon settings. Self-distillation can provide denser signals but may reinforce undesired behaviors if not carefully guided.

## [ComAct: COM-as-Action Paradigm for Software Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13239) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the COM-as-Action paradigm, reframing professional software manipulation as deterministic program synthesis instead of sequential visual control, and introduce ComCADBench, the first benchmark for agents operating real industrial CAD software. They also develop ComActor, a self-correcting agent trained via a progressive three-stage framework on the ComForge platform. This paradigm shift addresses fundamental limitations of GUI-based and API-based agents, such as fragile visual grounding and heterogeneous protocols, potentially enabling more reliable automation of professional software. The new benchmark and agent set a strong foundation for future research in software automation and AI agents. COM (Component Object Model) is a Microsoft binary-interface technology that allows language-neutral software component interaction. The COM-as-Action paradigm leverages COM to achieve deterministic program synthesis, and experiments show that COM-based execution yields substantial gains over GUI-based methods, with frontier models achieving near-zero success under GUI interaction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Component Object Model (COM) is a Microsoft technology that enables binary software components to interact in a language-neutral way, forming the foundation for OLE and ActiveX. Program synthesis is the task of automatically constructing a program from a high-level specification, and deterministic algorithms always produce the same output for a given input. GUI-based agents rely on visual grounding, which is fragile and error-prone, while API-based agents struggle with heterogeneous protocols and inaccessible interfaces.

## [EGLR: New Deterministic Axis for LLM Reasoning Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16620) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Entropy-Gated Latent Recursion (EGLR) introduces a deterministic layer-span sampling axis for inference-time scaling in LLMs, complementing stochastic token-level sampling. It re-applies top decoder layers at high-uncertainty tokens without training, creating an L×T Cartesian sampling space. EGLR addresses a fundamental limitation of current inference-time scaling methods that rely solely on stochastic sampling, offering a complementary deterministic axis that captures different problem subsets. This training-free approach can boost reasoning performance significantly, as shown by an 8.2 percentage point gain on MATH-500 with Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct. EGLR re-applies the top-L layers for at most K_max iterations until the next-token distribution converges, gated by entropy. It is training-free and compatible with any downstream procedure consuming rollouts, such as self-consistency, best-of-N, and GRPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Inference-time scaling improves LLM reasoning by allocating more compute during generation, typically through repeated stochastic sampling. However, diversity is limited to token-level randomness. EGLR introduces a second axis by recursively applying a subset of decoder layers, providing deterministic diversity that complements stochastic sampling.

## [Single-pass attention head readout beats Categorical Jacobian for protein contacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new method extracts protein contacts from language model attention heads in a single forward pass, matching or outperforming the Categorical Jacobian approach that requires about 19L forward passes. The method selects top-K contact-relevant heads using as few as 10 labeled proteins and averages their attention maps without any per-pair or per-head weights. This work dramatically reduces the computational cost of unsupervised protein contact prediction from language models, making it feasible for large-scale applications. It also reveals that contact information is already concentrated in a small subset of attention heads, providing mechanistic insight into how protein language models encode structure. On a leakage-clean CAMEO split, the head readout beats the Categorical Jacobian on ESM-2-650M by +9 percentage points (N=29, p<0.001). The method fails on causal language models, suggesting attention-encoded pair structure depends on bidirectional pretraining. The optimal number of heads K varies across architectures and correlates with how diffusely contact heads are spread.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Protein contact prediction aims to identify which pairs of amino acids are close in 3D space, crucial for structure prediction. The Categorical Jacobian method (Zhang et al., 2024) reads contacts by perturbing each residue with all 20 amino acids, requiring ~19L forward passes. ESM-2 is a family of bidirectional transformer protein language models trained on millions of sequences. Attention heads in transformers capture pairwise interactions between residues.

## [LLM Safety Refusal Is a Linear Feature, Not Deep Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22686) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces Contrastive Logit Steering (CLS), a zero-optimization method that isolates a linear 'refusal direction' in LLMs, showing that safety refusal is a manipulable linear feature rather than a deep semantic decision. CLS achieves high attack success rates (e.g., 95% on Llama-3.1) in about one second, and can also harden models against jailbreaks by inverting the steering vector. This research reveals a fundamental vulnerability in current safety alignment techniques, suggesting that alignment creates a steerable 'safety axis' that can be easily exploited. It also provides a precise primitive for defense, enabling bidirectional control of refusal behavior without retraining. CLS operates on the output logit distribution rather than internal activations, and when combined with prefix injection, it induces a phase transition where guardrails collapse. The method was tested across 7 model families, revealing architectural determinism: Llama-3.1 exhibits a 'Late Decision' topology easily bypassed, while Qwen-2.5 shows 'Early Divergence' with safety integrated mid-computation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are often fine-tuned with safety alignment to refuse harmful requests. Previous work on representation engineering has identified linear directions in hidden states that correspond to concepts, but CLS is the first to show that refusal itself is a linear feature at the logit level. Prefix injection is a known jailbreak technique that tricks the model into continuing a harmful response by providing an innocuous-looking prefix.

Researchers introduced TF-RefusalBench, a multilingual benchmark with 5,200 prompts derived from Swiss Supreme Court rulings, to measure and mitigate over-alignment in LLMs used for criminal law translation and summarization. They also demonstrated that abliteration (refusal directions ablation) can eliminate refusal with minimal impact on task performance. This work addresses a critical safety issue in legal AI: over-alignment causing LLMs to refuse legitimate tasks involving sensitive content. The benchmark and mitigation techniques enable safer deployment of LLMs in multilingual legal settings, balancing safety with utility. TF-RefusalBench covers four languages (French, German, Italian, English) and includes prompts likely to trigger refusal due to violent or sexual content descriptions. The study found that over-alignment is multifaceted, influenced by model, prompt language, and text language, and that disclaimers also harm task faithfulness.

## [PACE: Optimizing Iterate-Averaged Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25086) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose PACE, a lightweight wrapper around AdamW that pulls live weights toward their exponential moving average during training, improving the performance of the averaged model returned at the end. This addresses a fundamental mismatch in current LM pipelines where optimizers are designed for the final iterate but the averaged model is returned, potentially leading to significant gains in fine-tuning and pretraining of large language models. PACE uses a clipped, per-coordinate control strength derived from an optimal control formulation in a continuous-time stochastic quadratic model, and the authors prove convergence guarantees for a stylized version.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Many modern language model training pipelines return an exponential moving average (EMA) of the weights rather than the final weights, as EMA often yields better generalization. However, standard optimizers like AdamW are designed to optimize the final iterate, not the average. PACE reformulates optimizer design as an optimal control problem to bridge this gap.

<a id="item-203"></a>
## [Reflect-R1: Evidence-Driven Self-Correction for Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27922) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Reflect-R1 introduces a three-stage evidence-driven self-correction framework (intuition, verification, arbitration) for long video understanding, which dynamically retrieves objective visual evidence to break the hallucination loop. It also proposes a stage-decoupled reinforcement learning algorithm (SD-GRPO) to address policy coupling in multi-stage reflection. This work addresses critical limitations in multimodal reflection for long video understanding, where models often fail to correct errors due to lack of external evidence. By enabling authentic self-correction grounded in objective evidence, Reflect-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance on benchmarks like VideoMME and LongVideoBench, advancing AI's ability to reason over long-form video content. The framework consists of three stages: intuition (initial answer), verification (retrieving temporal visual evidence to check correctness), and arbitration (resolving conflicts via multiple temporal searches). The SD-GRPO algorithm independently computes advantage functions for each stage, and a 120K-sample dataset was constructed to bridge the training data gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Current multimodal reflection mechanisms for long video understanding rely on closed-loop self-reflection within internal parameters, lacking objective external evidence, which leads to blind confidence and failure to correct errors. Applying reinforcement learning to multi-stage reflection pipelines causes severe policy coupling, where policies across stages interfere with each other, exacerbated by scarcity of dedicated training data.

<a id="item-204"></a>
## [SHARD: Cell-Keyed Splitting for Privacy-Preserving Dense Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27976) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SHARD introduces a retrieval-preserving embedding transform that splits embeddings into a public prefix and private residual shards, each rotated under a separate secret key, to resist inversion and alignment attacks. The method uses CKKS homomorphic encryption for exact inner product reranking while keeping keys secret. This work addresses a fundamental weakness in current embedding protection schemes—single global geometry—which is vulnerable to alignment attacks like orthogonal Procrustes. SHARD's cell-keyed approach provides revocable, renewable, and unlinkable templates, significantly enhancing security for RAG systems and vector databases. The parameter C controls the number of cells, ranging from global-linear (C=1) to per-document micro-keys (C=N). Recovering the cell-keyed residual under a diffuse known-plaintext leak costs about C times more anchors (median 200 to 102,400 at C=256), and the public prefix leaks far less neighbor structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Dense embeddings are numerical vectors that represent text for semantic search and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). Attackers can invert these embeddings to recover original text or align them to known embeddings using methods like orthogonal Procrustes, which finds the best rotation between two point sets. SHARD defends by splitting embeddings into shards with per-cell secret rotations, making alignment infeasible without knowing all keys.

## [TISED Reveals Paradoxical Speed-Quality Trade-offs in Embodied AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28529) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TISED, a framework that decomposes the effects of lossy inference speedup techniques on embodied tasks, revealing three paradoxical findings: static tasks may take longer despite lower per-step latency, dynamic tasks can achieve higher success rates with moderate optimization, and the optimal trade-off shifts with hardware. This work challenges the conventional assumption that faster inference always improves task performance in embodied AI, providing a new perspective for designing efficient inference systems for robots and autonomous agents. TISED unifies techniques like quantization, pruning, and asynchronous inference, and analyzes their closed-loop effects on both static and dynamic embodied tasks. The framework shows that the monotonicity and sweet-spot of speed-quality trade-offs depend on hardware configuration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied AI involves agents that interact with the environment in a closed loop, where actions affect future observations. Lossy inference speedup techniques like quantization reduce per-step computation but may introduce errors. Traditional evaluation focuses on per-step latency, ignoring the cumulative closed-loop effects on task-level performance.

This approach addresses key limitations of VLM-based planning—geometric inaccuracy and lack of future awareness—potentially improving safety and reliability in end-to-end autonomous driving systems. ## [TraceLab: Characterizing Coding Agent Workloads for LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30560) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers released TraceLab, a trace of roughly 4,300 coding-agent sessions containing 350,000 LLM steps and 430,000 tool calls from real-world use of Claude Code and Codex, revealing workload patterns such as long autonomous loops, long contexts with short outputs, diverse tool calls, and high but imperfect prefix cache hit rates. This work fills a critical gap in understanding real-world coding agent workloads, providing actionable insights for optimizing LLM serving systems, such as lower-overhead tool calling and improved KV-cache management, which can directly impact the efficiency and cost of deploying agentic LLMs. The trace includes 430,000 tool calls with a heavily-tailed distribution, and prefix cache hit rates are high but imperfect, averaging around 70-80% depending on the workload. The dataset and analysis code are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are AI systems that autonomously write and debug code by interacting with LLMs in iterative loops, making tool calls (e.g., file reads, shell commands) along the way. Efficiently serving such agents requires understanding their unique workload patterns, which differ from traditional chat-based LLM usage. Prefix caching is a common optimization that reuses KV-cache blocks for repeated prompt prefixes to reduce computation.

## [IPFS Content Publishing 10x Faster with Optimistic Provide](https://probelab.io/blog/optimistic-provide/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IPFS content publishing is now up to 10x faster thanks to the Optimistic Provide algorithm, which returns control to the user after most RPCs succeed and handles the remaining ones asynchronously. This improvement significantly reduces the latency of publishing content on IPFS, making it more practical for real-world applications and improving user experience. The Optimistic Provide algorithm is a heuristic that trades off full consistency for speed, potentially making content retrieval slightly less reliable but greatly reducing publish time.

hackernews · dennis-tra · Jul 1, 15:30 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48748518)

**Background**: IPFS (InterPlanetary File System) is a peer-to-peer distributed file system that uses content addressing via CIDs. Publishing content involves storing key-value mappings in a Distributed Hash Table (DHT) to enable retrieval. The traditional provide process waits for all DHT PUT RPCs to complete, which can be slow due to network latency and peer churn.

## [Contrastive Reflection Optimizes Prompts for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30840) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new framework called Contrastive Reflection iteratively optimizes prompts for LLM agents in information retrieval by identifying error-anchored behavioral slices and using contrastive examples. On HotpotQA, one repair improved exact-match accuracy from 51.4% to 60.4%. This approach treats prompt optimization as debugging rather than blind search, making it more interpretable and validation-driven. It outperforms failure-only and random baselines, and competes with modern optimizers like MIPROv2 and GEPA. The framework uses a tree-based slice selector to find error-anchored slices, then adds nearby successful examples and asks a Teacher LLM to propose targeted edits. Edits are accepted only if validation performance improves, optionally with regression checks.

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used in information retrieval to issue queries, synthesize answers, and evaluate results. ## [Elderly Underrepresentation Biases Urban Mobility Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A study using Citi Bike data from Jersey City (2016-2020) reveals that elderly riders have distinct mobility patterns—smaller activity spaces, lower mobility entropy, and asymmetric off-peak timing—and that models trained on majority-dominated data systematically misrepresent elderly behavior, with Markov models overestimating step length by 4.5% and dwell time by 8.9%. This work highlights a critical fairness issue in mobility modeling: underrepresented groups like the elderly are systematically misrepresented, which can lead to biased urban planning and resource allocation. It underscores the need for demographic-aware data collection and model training to ensure equitable smart city development. The study compared a first-order Markov chain and a Qwen3-4B LLM fine-tuned with QLoRA across three training settings: full population, young riders only, and elderly riders only. Elderly-specific models achieved substantially lower errors, and higher-capability LLMs did not automatically improve subgroup fidelity under limited demographic data.

**Background**: Mobility modeling uses trajectory data to understand and predict human movement patterns, often for urban planning and transportation. Synthetic trajectory generation creates artificial movement paths that mimic real-world patterns. Mobility entropy measures the unpredictability of a person's movements; lower entropy indicates more routine behavior. The Citi Bike System Data is a public dataset of bike-sharing trips in New York City and Jersey City.

## [Budgeted Environment Probing for World-Model Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes a budgeted probing operator that allows language agents to proactively query the environment to correct world-model drift within a limited number of steps, improving decision-making in long-horizon tasks. This addresses a critical practical problem in deploying language agents for extended tasks, where world-model drift can cause failures that are only detected later. By treating environment interaction as a scarce calibration resource, the method enables more reliable autonomous agents. The method introduces a type-stratified analysis that distinguishes procedural beliefs (e.g., tool dependencies) from spatial beliefs (e.g., object locations), and shows that targeted probes can reduce terminal world-model error when the probe policy follows the task structure.

**Background**: Language agents maintain an internal world model to track state across decisions, but this model can drift over time due to incomplete information or environmental changes. The paper frames environment probing as a calibration step that consumes task steps, creating a trade-off between exploration and task progress.

## [Who Decides the Meaning of Your Emotion?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31442) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces the concept of 'affective sovereignty' by decomposing measurement uncertainty in emotion-sensing AI, showing that irreducible meaning uncertainty prevents AI from having final authority over an individual's emotion. This work challenges the prevailing accuracy-centric paradigm in emotion AI, arguing that interpretive authority should belong to the experiencing subject rather than the system, which has direct implications for design, evaluation, and regulation of affective technologies. The paper defines a 'meaning distribution' as the distribution of labels from annotators under a fixed protocol, and decomposes its uncertainty into reducible and irreducible components, demonstrating that the irreducible component cannot be estimated with adequate coverage under realistic annotator counts.

CSTrader is a multi-agent framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to trade Counter-Strike 2 (CS2) weapon skins, integrating heterogeneous signals and specialized agents for technical analysis, liquidity, events, and reversed sentiment. In a simulated environment with real data from a volatile period, it achieved up to 7.58% cumulative return, outperforming both a falling market index (-15.62%) and simple single-prompt LLM baselines. This work demonstrates that LLMs can effectively turn unstructured text into profitable trading actions in niche, language-driven markets, which are challenging for traditional quantitative models. It provides a new benchmark for language-to-action research and highlights the potential of multi-agent LLM frameworks for volatile asset markets. The framework includes specialized agents for technical analysis, liquidity, events, and reversed sentiment, plus risk control, transaction friction, and portfolio management agents. Ablation studies revealed that liquidity, reversed sentiment, and transaction friction agents are crucial for stable profits from noisy language signals.

By using zero-shot foundation models, CLOUDADV reduces the need for per-tenant retraining and revalidation, making cost optimization more scalable and easier to deploy in dynamic cloud environments. The system uses a higher-capacity LLM offline to generate reference recommendations and a smaller production model for deployment, evaluating alignment under latency and cost constraints. It incorporates historical utilization, forecast summaries, VM metadata, pricing, and sizing heuristics into a structured decision context for each query.

## [ACE: Pluggable Adaptive Context Elasticizer for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31564) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ACE, a plug-and-play module that adaptively manages historical context for LLM agents by dynamically assigning each step a type of raw, abstract, or drop at every decision step. ACE addresses the critical limitation of fixed context windows in LLM agents by enabling reversible context compression, which prevents irreversible loss of important information during long trajectories. This could significantly improve performance on multi-step agent tasks without requiring model retraining. ACE maintains a lossless message maintenance layer storing both raw messages and compressed abstractions, while a context orchestration layer adaptively assigns elastic types. It was successfully adapted to four agent frameworks (ReAct, DeepAgent, WebThinker, MiroFlow) without training or architectural changes, consistently outperforming truncation and summarization baselines.

This work addresses the open problem of dense volumetric cloud reconstruction from sparse ground-based instruments, achieving state-of-the-art accuracy in liquid water content and wind speed estimation, which could improve weather forecasting and climate modeling. AtmoFuseNet achieves 0.026 g/m³ MAE for liquid water content and 1.18 m/s MAE for wind speed on collocated observations from a semi-arid site, with ablation experiments confirming the contribution of each module.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Cloud microphysical properties like liquid water content are critical for weather and climate models but are difficult to measure directly. Ground-based instruments such as sky cameras, cloud radars, and ceilometers provide complementary but sparse observations. Cross-modal attention mechanisms allow the model to dynamically align features from different modalities, while variational refinement enforces physical consistency with forward models.

Researchers developed a user-centered interactive machine learning (UC-iML) framework that integrates physician-guided feature refinement with SHAP explanations to improve delirium detection in hospitalized patients, using data from 3,862 admissions across six Toronto hospitals. Delirium is often missed in routine care, and this framework demonstrates a practical human-in-the-loop approach that leverages clinical expertise to build more robust and interpretable models, potentially improving patient outcomes and trust in AI-assisted diagnosis. The framework uses temporally separated holdout testing and a later-phase validation cohort to assess temporal robustness, and SHAP values are employed to summarize feature attribution, highlighting clinically meaningful signals.

SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is a game-theoretic method that explains model predictions by attributing importance to input features. The GEMINI dataset collects detailed clinical data from multiple hospitals in Ontario, Canada, enabling large-scale research.

## [AI Transparency: Compliance vs. Stakeholder Needs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30652) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study analyzes 92 AI transparency statements from Australian government agencies and introduces the Risk-Control-Involvement-Need (RCIN) framework to evaluate whether mandated transparency actually serves stakeholder needs. This research exposes a 'Transparency Illusion' where compliance with disclosure mandates does not guarantee meaningful transparency for high-risk, low-control stakeholders, challenging current AI governance practices. The RCIN framework differentiates stakeholder classes based on risk exposure, decision control, and involvement, and the study finds that criteria for high-control stakeholders are well met, while those for high-risk, low-control groups are often neglected.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Governments increasingly mandate transparency statements for public-sector AI systems, but these are often treated as an end in themselves. From a requirements engineering perspective, compliance with disclosure criteria does not automatically validate that stakeholder needs are met. The RCIN framework provides a structured way to assess transparency adequacy.

## [Local Pheromone Network: Sparse Local Learning Without Backprop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30669) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Local Pheromone Network introduces a sparse, local Hebbian learning rule with multi-scale synaptic traces, adaptive budgets, and optional replay, enabling continual learning without backpropagation. This work addresses key limitations of backpropagation, such as catastrophic forgetting and parameter coupling, potentially advancing neuromorphic computing and continual learning systems. Each synapse stores a weight, short-term and long-term pheromone traces, and an optional consolidation state; the update budget shrinks when loss improves and expands toward active neighborhoods when loss worsens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Backpropagation is the dominant training method for deep neural networks but suffers from catastrophic forgetting and global parameter coupling. Hebbian learning, inspired by neuroscience, updates weights based on local co-activity without global error signals. The Local Pheromone Network draws inspiration from ant colony pheromone trails to implement multi-scale temporal traces for local learning.

## [SDD Frameworks Trade Determinism for Verifiability in LLM Code Gen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30689) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study compares three Spec-Driven Development (SDD) frameworks—traceSDD, Spec Kit, and OpenSpec—across two frontier LLMs (Claude Sonnet 4.6 and GLM-5-turbo), measuring output determinism and automated hallucination detection rate (TDR). The study finds that citation annotations reduce determinism but enable high hallucination detection (86-88% TDR), while uncited conditions yield higher determinism but zero detection. This study provides rigorous evidence of a fundamental trade-off in LLM-based code generation: requiring per-line requirement citations improves verifiability and hallucination detection but reduces output determinism. The cross-model replication suggests this trade-off is general, guiding practitioners in choosing SDD frameworks based on their priorities. The study involved 240 implementations with Claude Sonnet 4.6 and 600 with GLM-5-turbo across four conditions. traceSDD (cited) significantly outperformed Spec Kit on determinism (Cohen's d=0.47 for Claude, 0.42 for GLM) but not OpenSpec. The automated hallucination detection achieved 86.4% TDR for Claude and 88.0% for GLM with 0% false positive rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Spec-Driven Development (SDD) is a software methodology where a formal specification serves as the authoritative source for implementation, testing, and documentation. The three frameworks studied differ in traceability: traceSDD enforces per-line citations with hierarchical REQ-XXX.Y.Z identifiers, Spec Kit uses artifact-level traceability via user stories, and OpenSpec uses post-hoc external trace maps. Output determinism measures lexical similarity across independent LLM sessions, while TDR (True Detection Rate) measures the fraction of hallucinated code lines correctly flagged.

## [Stationary-Distribution Theory for Triplet Plateau Search in Random Forests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper develops a stationary-distribution theory for triplet-based plateau search in Random Forest ensemble-size selection, modeling the central ensemble size as a birth-death Markov chain on a geometric grid and deriving its stationary distribution through local balance. This work reframes plateau-based Random Forest tuning as a stochastic process rather than a deterministic stopping rule, providing rigorous theoretical foundations that could improve hyperparameter optimization in ensemble methods. Under a folded-normal approximation, the stationary center B* scales as O(ε^{-2}) as ε→0, and the stationary spread has variance O(ε^{-4}) with a leading relative spread independent of ε.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Random Forests are ensemble methods that combine many decision trees; the number of trees is a key hyperparameter that affects both accuracy and computational cost. Plateau-based tuning adapts the ensemble size by comparing out-of-bag scores at a geometric triplet of tree counts, but the process can fluctuate rather than converge deterministically. Birth-death Markov chains model transitions where states increase or decrease by one, and the folded-normal distribution describes the absolute value of a normal random variable.

This framework provides a standardized way to isolate and measure the effect of file-level localization, a critical upstream step in repository-level repair, helping researchers identify bottlenecks and improve end-to-end systems. The empirical results confirm that better localization directly boosts repair effectiveness and reduces latency, guiding future research directions. Loc2Repair uses three repair backbones on SWE-bench Verified, comparing baseline repair without explicit localization, repair guided by predicted localization from two localizers, and repair guided by gold modified-file sets. Pooled performance increased from 44.7% to 48.9% and 49.1% with predicted localization, and to 52.4% with gold localization; mean elapsed time decreased by up to 154.45 seconds.

The paper introduces Q-Margin, a novel α-divergence loss function for face and speaker verification that encodes margin penalties into prior probabilities instead of logits, inducing sparsity and discriminative embeddings. Q-Margin achieves competitive or superior performance on IJB-B, IJB-C, and VoxCeleb benchmarks, especially improving low False Acceptance Rates critical for high-security applications, while enabling memory-efficient training due to extreme sparsity. Unlike conventional margin-penalty losses like ArcFace and CosFace that apply geometric penalties to logits, Q-Margin encodes the margin into the reference measure (prior probabilities) within the α-divergence framework, preserving sparsity when α>1.

Q-Margin bridges this gap by integrating margins into the probabilistic framework.

A new paper proposes using sparse autoencoders (SAEs) solely as semantic detectors for object erasure in diffusion models, replacing detected patch embeddings instead of directly steering latent space, which avoids visual artifacts. This work reveals a fundamental gap between concept detection and intervention in diffusion models, positioning SAEs as powerful interpretability tools but cautioning against their use as direct control knobs for unlearning. The detection-based replacement method preserves the diffusion model's activation statistics, producing significantly cleaner erasure results than latent steering. The paper systematically evaluates SAEs for object erasure and steering, showing direct latent intervention frequently induces out-of-distribution activations.

Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising latent representations. Concept erasure aims to remove unwanted content (e.g., objects) from generated images, often by modifying model weights or activations. Previous work assumed SAE features could serve as controllable intervention points, but this assumption is challenged by the new findings.

FLORA is a deep learning framework that uses octree-based processing and auxiliary data to predict six forest attributes from heterogeneous LiDAR point clouds, achieving an rRMSE of about 12.3% for dominant height and 39% for total volume. This work addresses the challenge of making robust forest attribute predictions from LiDAR data acquired under varying conditions, which is critical for national forest inventories as LiDAR programs expand across Europe. FLORA combines an octree-based backbone with ecological and spatiotemporal auxiliary variables via a late-fusion gating mechanism, and was trained on 32,052 NFI plots across France using data from the French LiDAR HD program.

Octree-based processing recursively partitions 3D space into octants, enabling efficient handling of sparse and irregular point cloud data.

## [KCR Framework Resolves Knowledge Conflicts in LLMs via RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose the Knowledge Conflict Reasoning (KCR) framework that disentangles conflicting contexts into reasoning traces using a hybrid text-graph representation and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). A 7B model enhanced by KCR outperforms GPT-4o and GPT-5.1 on complex conflict resolution tasks. Explicit knowledge conflicts are a critical challenge for LLMs as they integrate diverse data sources, and KCR provides a systematic method to resolve them, significantly improving reasoning reliability. This could lead to more robust AI systems in retrieval-augmented generation and other applications. KCR first disentangles conflicting contexts into discrete reasoning traces using a hybrid text-graph representation, then applies RLVR to learn a reasoning policy that maximizes logical consistency. The framework is evaluated on complex tasks, showing that a 7B model with KCR surpasses leading proprietary models like GPT-4o and GPT-5.1.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Explicit knowledge conflicts occur when retrieved contexts contain contradictory information, which is common in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. LLMs often struggle to resolve such conflicts due to entangled narratives and heterogeneous conflict patterns. Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a training paradigm where the model receives rewards only when responses meet verification criteria, enabling it to learn from free exploration.

Researchers propose a novel framework that integrates large language models (LLMs) with multi-agent deep reinforcement learning and a dynamic Stackelberg game to synthesize adaptive MAC protocols for wireless networks, achieving 77.6% higher throughput and 65.2% fairness improvement over baselines. ## [AI-Assisted Search Improves Hypercube Slicing Bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16807) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers have improved the upper bound for slicing the n-dimensional hypercube with hyperplanes from 5n/6 to 4n/5, using an AI-assisted search tool called CPro1. The new bound shows that S(n) ≤ ⌈4n/5⌉, except when n is an odd multiple of 5, where S(n) ≤ 4n/5 + 1. This result improves a 50-year-old upper bound for a classic combinatorial geometry problem, demonstrating the power of AI-assisted search in mathematical discovery. The techniques may inspire further advances in discrete geometry and combinatorial optimization. The improvement was achieved by constructing 8 hyperplanes that slice all edges of the 10-dimensional hypercube Q10, using a local search algorithm enhanced by CPro1. CPro1 is an automatic tool that uses reasoning LLMs and automated hyperparameter tuning to generate search algorithms for mathematical constructions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: The hypercube slicing problem asks for the minimum number of hyperplanes needed to intersect every edge of an n-dimensional hypercube. This problem has been studied since the 1970s, with the previous best upper bound of 5n/6 due to Paterson in 1971. The new bound of 4n/5 represents a significant step forward.

This paper introduces Diffusion Crossover, a method that defines evolutionary recombination in diffusion models by interpolating noise sequences via spherical linear interpolation (Slerp) to produce offspring that inherit traits from parent images. This work bridges evolutionary computation and generative AI, enabling semantically consistent crossover in high-dimensional generative spaces, which could significantly enhance interactive evolutionary computation for creative design and optimization. ## [Optimal Subgoal Persistence in Hierarchical Latent Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03741) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends the Hierarchical Reasoning Model (HRM) with a feudal-style manager-worker interface, introducing a subgoal persistence period P that controls how often the high-level module emits a new subgoal. Experiments on ARC and ConceptARC show that moderate persistence (P=3-6) balances stability and adaptivity, achieving the best performance. This work addresses a fundamental tradeoff in long-horizon reasoning: how often to re-plan. The findings provide a design principle for building compositional planning systems that operate in latent space, which is crucial for scaling AI reasoning to complex, multi-step tasks. The optimal subgoal persistence period was found to be P=3, achieving a minimum LM loss of 1.544 compared to 1.674 at P=1 and a baseline of 1.640, with results replicated over 5 seeds (mean 1.595, std 0.045). The intrinsic alignment weight lambda also shows a narrow optimum around 0.05.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Hierarchical latent reasoning uses a slow high-level module to emit subgoals that guide a fast low-level module's hidden-state updates, enabling multi-step planning without external token traces. The stability-adaptivity tradeoff refers to the need to commit to a plan long enough for structure to form, but not so long that the plan becomes outdated.

This addresses a key challenge in DRL interpretability by providing faithful, human-intuitive explanations of internal representations, which is crucial for trust in high-stakes applications like autonomous driving and robotics. The framework introduces a value-sensitive discretization mechanism that uses the agent's value function to transform continuous state features into interpretable atomic concepts, bridging the gap between continuous signals and symbolic reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) combines deep neural networks with reinforcement learning to solve complex control tasks, but the resulting policies are often opaque. Concept-based methods have been successful in computer vision for interpreting neural networks, but applying them to DRL is difficult because continuous state spaces lack predefined semantic concepts. This work proposes a novel approach to automatically discover such concepts from the agent's value function.

The algorithm uses an uncertainty interval of the self-consistency classifier to decide when to invoke the verifier model, and the paper provides a geometric interpretation via kernel mean embeddings.

## [Reproducible Benchmark of Lightweight CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03303) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new reproducible benchmark compares seven lightweight CNNs on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet under a unified fine-tuning protocol, finding EfficientNetV2-S achieves the highest accuracy while EfficientNet-B0 offers a better efficiency-accuracy trade-off. This benchmark provides a reliable reference for practitioners selecting lightweight CNNs, addressing the common issue of incomparable results due to differing training recipes and input settings. EfficientNetV2-S achieves top-1 accuracies of 97.57%, 86.98%, and 78.73% on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet respectively, while EfficientNet-B0 uses only 21% of its parameters and 14% of its multiply-accumulate operations on Tiny ImageNet yet stays within 0.85 percentage points in accuracy.

RoMaP introduces a robust 3D mask generation module (3D-GALP) using spherical harmonics for consistent multi-view part segmentation, and a regularized SDS loss with an L1 anchor loss via Scheduled Latent Mixing and Part (SLaMP) editing to enable precise part-level modifications in 3D Gaussian Splatting. This work addresses key limitations in local 3D editing—inconsistent multi-view segmentations and ambiguous SDS loss—making part-level editing more robust and flexible, which is crucial for 3D content creation and neural rendering applications. The 3D-GALP module uses spherical harmonics coefficients to model view-dependent label variations and soft-label properties, while the regularized SDS loss includes an L1 anchor loss and Gaussian prior removal to confine edits to target regions and preserve context.

This paper introduces a method to construct a textual dataset for training LLMs on analog circuit knowledge, using a multi-agent framework to generate structured question-thinking-solution-answer (QTSA) quadruples. This paper introduces the Neighborhood-Contextualized Message-Passing (NCMP) framework, which generalizes standard message-passing GNNs by incorporating full local neighborhood context beyond pairwise interactions. The authors also propose a concrete instantiation, SINC-GCN, which achieves strong expressivity with efficient runtime. NCMP addresses a fundamental limitation of classical GNNs—their inability to capture rich neighborhood context—potentially improving performance on graph learning tasks. The proposed SINC-GCN demonstrates that neighborhood contextualization can yield substantial gains without heavy computational overhead, making it practical for real-world applications. The NCMP framework leverages set-based aggregation and a key property of attentional GNNs to incorporate multiset neighborhood context. SINC-GCN, a simple parametrization of NCMP, maintains asymptotic runtime complexity comparable to standard GNNs while outperforming baselines on synthetic and benchmark datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are neural networks designed for graph-structured data, using message-passing to update node representations by aggregating information from neighbors. Standard message-passing GNNs typically compute pairwise messages between a center node and each neighbor individually, ignoring the overall structure of the neighborhood. This limitation can hinder their ability to distinguish certain graph structures, motivating methods that consider the entire local subgraph.

This paper proposes a sequence truncation method for knowledge distillation of reasoning models, showing that training on only the first 50% of tokens retains about 91% of full-sequence performance while reducing training time, memory, and FLOPs by roughly 50%. This approach significantly reduces the computational cost of distilling large reasoning models into smaller ones, making it more practical for deployment on resource-constrained devices. It also provides insights into the allocation of supervision across different parts of reasoning sequences. The method selectively distills only the chain-of-thought (CoT) tokens, as they encompass the prompt and answer information. The truncation protocol quantifies computation-quality tradeoffs, showing that beyond a certain length, longer sequences yield marginal returns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model. Chain-of-thought reasoning involves generating intermediate reasoning steps to improve complex reasoning in large language models. This work combines both concepts to improve efficiency.

This review analyzes 75 studies from 2017-2025, categorizing SSL methods into four paradigms and examining how pretext-task design aligns with downstream clinical objectives in medical image analysis. It provides practical design guidelines for selecting SSL strategies based on imaging modality and task type, helping researchers and clinicians reduce reliance on expensive annotations. The four paradigms are contrastive, non-contrastive/predictive, generative/reconstruction-based, and hybrid learning; contrastive methods suit classification but may miss subtle pathology, while reconstruction-based methods preserve anatomical structure for segmentation.

## [Topological Neural Dynamics: Neuron-Wise Sequence Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21295) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Topological Neural Dynamics (TND) introduces a sequence modeling framework where each neuron evolves independently via a directed graph, replacing the traditional layer-wise dynamics used in RNNs, LSTMs, and Transformers. This shift from layer-wise to neuron-wise dynamics could provide a more effective inductive bias for sequence modeling, as demonstrated by TND achieving over three times the catch rate of the strongest baseline in a Pong behavior cloning task. TND models a neural system as a directed neuron graph, an interaction operator, and a local dynamics function, with collective computation emerging from interactions through the graph topology. It was evaluated on a behavior cloning task in single-player Pong, achieving a mean of 17.47 consecutive catches per round.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional sequence models like RNNs, LSTMs, and Transformers update all neurons in a layer through a shared parameterized operator, coupling their temporal evolution. In contrast, many complex dynamical systems exhibit rich global behavior from locally evolving units interacting through structured connectivity. TND draws inspiration from this principle to propose neuron-wise dynamics.

The paper introduces BREIT, a modular framework for 3D multi-frequency electrical impedance tomography (MF-EIT) stroke reconstruction, including a neuroimaging-to-EIT pipeline, a forward solver, and a 3D D-bar implementation. It also proposes dFNO-bar, a novel deep-learning method that integrates Fourier Neural Operators into the D-bar reconstruction algorithm. BREIT addresses the lack of large-scale datasets and standardized pipelines in 3D deep-learning EIT stroke reconstruction, potentially accelerating research and clinical translation. The dFNO-bar method demonstrates improved image quality (higher brain SSIM) compared to existing methods, which could enhance stroke diagnosis and monitoring. The framework includes a self-contained Python 3D Complete Electrode Model (CEM) forward solver for simulating MF-EIT voltages and supports non-uniform electrode layouts. dFNO-bar learns a mapping from scattering data to conductivity, and was evaluated against D-bar, Deep D-bar, and Gauss-Newton methods on UCLH-matched synthetic data, showing higher brain SSIM with comparable CC across noise settings.

## [Hybrid Shallow Network for Explainable Cybersecurity Risk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30953) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose the Neuro-Bayesian-Symbolic Residual Attention Shallow Network (NBS-RASN), a shallow neural network with 80 interpretable neurons across 12 layers that enforces five epistemological axioms as hard constraints for explainable cybersecurity risk assessment. This work challenges the assumption that deep learning requires deep networks, showing that shallow networks with deep reasoning can outperform opaque models in high-stakes cybersecurity where interpretability is essential. The network produces fully decomposable scores consisting of a deterministic weighted component plus an expert adjustment, with each adjustment traceable to named amplifiers such as blast radius and propagation speed. It was validated on 20 open-source projects covering all OWASP Top 10:2025 categories, achieving confidence scores between 0.79 and 0.97.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 1, 04:00

**Background**: Explainable AI (XAI) aims to make machine learning models transparent and interpretable, especially in high-stakes domains like cybersecurity. Traditional deep neural networks often sacrifice interpretability for accuracy, becoming black boxes. The proposed hybrid architecture combines neural, Bayesian, and symbolic components to maintain interpretability without sacrificing performance.

Researchers distilled knowledge from the large reasoning model DeepSeek-R1 into the compact Qwen2.5-7B model using math competition problems, achieving only a 4.76 percentage-point accuracy improvement and revealing severe overfitting. The exact terms of the deal with the government remain undisclosed, and it is unclear whether access to Fable will be extended to subscribers or require API pricing immediately.

The lifting of restrictions follows negotiations between Anthropic and the Trump administration.
